## location-image-cache — reuse maps for revisited locations for 30 days

Outcome: reuse a previously generated map when the vehicle returns near its saved location within 30 days. This planning update supersedes the older latest-image-only/exact-coordinate cache requirement. Non-goals: implementation or deployment during this planning turn, changing GPS polling, app installation, release publication, or caching vehicle authentication. Files for this turn: C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\plan.md only.

### cache-locked-decisions — preserve the requested retention policy

- 2026-10-24, user-confirmed verbatim: "let's add a 30 day cache by location, so if we're at the same location, it re-uses previoulsy generated images, we can use a 30 day ttl on the dynamodb table, and how do we fuzzy match location, within what 50 or 100 ft?"
- 2026-10-24: the owner accepted the preceding recommendation with "excellent add that". Use a 50-foot (15.24-metre) matching radius and a cached background with the latest reported position drawn separately. The pin reflects the accuracy of the source fix, not guaranteed physical precision.
- Accepted expiry: 30 days from successful image generation, not a sliding 30 days from each hit. Reuse must not preserve old imagery indefinitely.

### cache-verified-facts — distinguish current behavior from the planned cache

- Read 2026-10-24: C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\internal\locationmap\service.go uses exact latitude/longitude equality in same(), keeps only the latest RenderedFix, and skips Render/Publish only for that match. It does not implement revisited-location lookup.
- Read 2026-10-24: C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\template.yaml configures DynamoDB TTL through expiresAt. Existing encrypted location records use 90-day expiry; map-cache records need their own 30-day expiry without changing history retention.
- AWS TTL deletion is asynchronous and may take days. Lookup must reject expiresAt <= current UTC time even while the record still exists. Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/TTL.html . DynamoDB TTL does not remove the corresponding S3 image.

### location-cache-contract — define matching, expiry, and private image reuse

depends on: pause-owner-resume, approved Amazon Location integration; provider storage terms resolved before implementation.

- [ ] cache-radius-decision — use the accepted 50-foot maximum distance. Compare actual ground distance from the latest fix to each saved image centre with the Haversine distance formula; choose the nearest unexpired match. Resolve equal distances by newest generation time. Do not compare rounded coordinates alone or move the saved centre on a cache hit, which would permit cumulative drift.
- [ ] cache-location-index — use bounded DynamoDB key queries for the location's spatial cell and intersecting neighbouring cells, then apply exact distance filtering. Cover cell boundaries and paginate within an explicit bound; never use a DynamoDB Scan on serving paths. Reuse the existing encrypted-document patterns; raw coordinates must not appear in keys, logs, object names, or public responses. Final key layout belongs to implementation review, not this planning change.
- [ ] cache-render-identity — scope matches to owner/vehicle and identical provider, style, zoom, dimensions, theme, label/POI settings, overlays and renderer version. M71 is an example, not a confirmed style selection. A changed visual configuration must not retrieve an incompatible image.
- [ ] cache-thirty-day-expiry — set cache metadata expiresAt to generatedAt + 2,592,000 seconds. Check expiry in application reads, including the current image, and treat a missing object as a cache miss. Keep image bytes in private S3 and metadata in DynamoDB. Add a 30-day S3 lifecycle rule for the dedicated cache prefix; do not apply it to unrelated objects. Only the stream-triggered image resolver generates replacements. Each scheduled observation, even at unchanged coordinates, triggers expiry evaluation.
- [ ] cache-current-delivery — on a hit, reuse the saved image through the existing protected current-map path with zero Amazon map calls and zero background-map rendering; drawing the latest pin remains local image composition. Record fresh observation/poll time separately from original image-generation time. Use resolver idempotency and per-cache-entry generation ownership so concurrent events cannot generate duplicate images or publish an older result over a newer one. App/widget retrieval remains storage-only.
- [ ] cache-marker-truth — cache the background without a vehicle pin. Retain its projection, bounds, dimensions and fixed centre so the latest reported fix can be projected onto the correct pixel. The app shared map renderer draws the latest position over the protected background; the full widget uses that same renderer at its default view. The resolver does not bake the marker into the cached image. This does not call the map provider. Preserve attribution and require the pin to fit within the usable image; otherwise treat the background as a cache miss.
- [x] cache-provider-storage — 2026-10-24: owner confirms an AWS exception allowing 30-day caching, resolving the earlier standard-terms concern for this planned use. Keep the fixed 30-day image expiry and private storage design. Do not infer a longer retention period, permission for other customers/providers or removal of attribution. This marks the permission decision complete, not the cache implementation. Standard attribution remains required: https://aws.amazon.com/service-terms/ section 82.10.
- [ ] cache-regression-proof — extend C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\internal\locationmap\service_test.go for A-to-B-to-A reuse, 49/50/51-foot boundaries, adjacent spatial cells, nearest-match selection, fixed-centre drift protection, incompatible style/size rejection, expiry at exactly 30 days despite an undeleted DynamoDB item, missing S3 objects, and concurrent worker publication. Assert provider/render calls are zero on a hit and bounded on a miss; no real vehicle requests.

Proof after implementation: from C:\dev\Suburban-Remote, `go test ./internal/locationmap ./internal/web -count=1 -timeout=120s` must exit 0 with the listed regression cases present; `go run ./cmd/verify template --schema-baseline infra/deployed-schema.yaml` must exit 0. This documentation-only turn uses `git diff --check -- plan.md`; no runtime behavior is claimed complete.

### location-event-pipeline — separate collection from image resolution

depends on: pause-owner-resume, location-cache-contract.

- 2026-10-24, user-confirmed verbatim: "excellent add that, we also may be adding an addtional source, so I want one lambda pulling the location, and when the location is put into the dynamodb table, it fires off the lambda to find the current iamge and either update the ddynamodb row with the index of new version, or the index of a cached image."
- This supersedes older text assigning map generation to the scheduled GPS worker. Location ingestion writes observations; DynamoDB Streams invokes a separate image resolver. A potential second source is a stated interface requirement, not authorization to connect an unspecified provider.
- [ ] location-collector-boundary — one location-collection Lambda handles the existing approved 30-minute polling path and writes a normalized, encrypted observation row. Include owner/vehicle, source name, source observation identity, observedAt (or explicitly unknown), receivedAt and position. It must not call Amazon Maps, render images or wait for image resolution. Preserve existing vehicle leases, authorization, cooldown and wake limits.
- [ ] location-source-contract — all future authorized sources write the same observation contract with their own source identity. Deduplicate by source and observation identity. Each accepted poll gets an observation event even if coordinates are unchanged. No new provider implementation or schedule now. Before activating a second source, record source priority and freshness/accuracy rules; preserve provenance and never silently let an older source fix replace a newer current fix.
- [ ] location-stream-trigger — enable DynamoDB Streams with the images required to compare old/new records. Trigger only normalized location INSERTs or changes to location revision, never cache rows, resolver-only updates, or TTL REMOVE events. The handler repeats these checks rather than trusting event filtering alone, preventing its own row update from creating a loop. Reuse encrypted persistence; no raw coordinates in stream logs or failures.
- [ ] location-image-resolver — read the observation, query the 30-day/50-foot cache for the matching render configuration, and reuse or generate the background. Store immutable private image objects before publishing references. Update the SAME observation row conditionally with opaque imageId, imageVersion, cacheHit, imageGeneratedAt and ready/failed status. Retain the background reference and authenticated projection/position metadata for client-side marker drawing; do not generate a separate server-composed image for every observation. An index means a stable image reference, not a mutable array offset or public URL.
- [ ] location-resolution-ordering — treat stream delivery as at least once. Duplicate events must reuse a completed result; concurrent misses must share one generation claim. Compare observation identity/revision on row updates. Update the current-location pointer only if the observation is still current; late completion may finish its own historical row but cannot replace the current map. Missing/expired images remain misses. On failure retain truthful unavailable/stale status, never mark ready without a readable object.
- [ ] location-resolver-recovery — use partial batch failure reporting and bisect failed batches. Configure a maximum of two event-source retries and a maximum record age of one hour, then retain the failed event reference in a private on-failure destination with no raw location logging. No nested application retry loop. Deterministic failures require a fix before replay; replay uses the same observation identity and is idempotent. Before implementation, record the exact deployed resource names and one-event replay command in this plan. Do not create CloudWatch alarms or dashboards; all new log groups retain seven days.
- [ ] location-expiry-separation — retain the existing 90-day observation-history policy. Apply the 30-day TTL only to image-cache metadata, with S3 lifecycle cleanup for corresponding background images. A historical row may outlive its image; resolve that as expired/unavailable rather than a working image link. Current map delivery must also enforce image expiry.
- [ ] location-pipeline-proof — extend existing tests for collector success without renderer availability, normalized source provenance, stream-triggered cache hit/miss, resolver update without recursive invocation, duplicate event, concurrent miss, older completion, expiry with unchanged coordinates, partial batch failure and terminal failure retention. Require mocked vehicle/map providers; no live vehicle actuation.

Expected implementation files: existing C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\internal\locationmap service/tests and encrypted persistence helpers, collector/worker wiring, a dedicated Go image-resolver Lambda entry point, C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\template.yaml and existing deployment verification. Add only the requested second execution boundary; no generic provider framework. Definition of done after implementation: `go test ./internal/locationmap ./internal/worker ./internal/web -count=1 -timeout=120s` and `go run ./cmd/verify template --schema-baseline infra/deployed-schema.yaml` exit 0, and a CI-owned synthetic observation proves exactly one resolution with no self-trigger loop. Record the exact CI proof command before execution. The current turn changes plan.md only.

## review-dispositions - known issues and concrete prevention

- Widget mismatch: separate layouts are rejected. One shared scene plus adapter pixel/hitbox comparison is now mandatory; visual parity is not marked complete.
- Premature install: candidate/test installation is explicitly inside the release hold. Add fail-closed readiness gates before any further delivery; successful signing is insufficient.
- Preview confusion: keep Live/Preview distinct, label fixtures, restore temporary test mode when authorized, and never treat example data as live proof.
- Unknown request loss: source fix retains unknown identity and blocks repeats, with read-only recheck. Preserve regression coverage through renderer changes and runtime verification.
- Startup permissions: request applicable missing OS permissions on startup; preserve session restoration around permission dialogs. The owner reports prior Amazon login, so investigate before requesting it again.
- Session/notification races: source fixes defer SYNC until lock release and sync fresh restores; cold cached refresh does not invent a sign-in requirement. Preserve tests and verify the real authenticated path later.
- Map failure: bounded source budget corrected and tested; real source still returns502. Budget exhausted, real image not delivered, no silent fallback or extra wake.
- Key ownership: raw key now stored only in the named SSM SecureString; digest configuration exists but live activation remains pending. No more requests for the operator to create or paste a key.
- Accessibility: the owner waived this requirement on2026-10-24. Historical findings remain for accuracy, but accessibility is no longer a release blocker and needs no framework change.
- Reporting: computer-wide chat/email rules now enforce short bullets, under50-word status items, under50-word blockers, requested-only technical evidence and concrete prevention after mistakes. Internal verification receipts remain mandatory.
- Workspace safety: preserve unrelated changes and use focused isolated-index delivery. Automatic approval review rejected cleanup of task-created temporary probes/snapshots with no stated reason; those files remain. Do not retry deletion through another mechanism.

## Stop conditions (only these)

- Current owner-requested pause. After plan preservation and the requested email, stop all agents and task processes. No automatic continuation or new schedule.
- App installation/publication while requirements are unfinished or release hold remains active. No exception for candidate/test builds.
- Confirmed authentication failure after diagnosis, or an unavailable approved map source, blocks only its dependent gate after an explicit resume. Prior owner sign-in is not assumed missing. Accessibility is excluded from the gates. Never invent successful proof.
- No vehicle actuation without separate current owner readiness; no replay of an uncertain action. No raw credentials, coordinates/history or key-bearing URLs in outputs.
- Preserve the exhausted three-render diagnostic ceiling. Other CI fixes allow at most three attempts per diagnosed fix, with terminal success/failure/cancellation/timeout tracking; no identical deterministic retry.
- A material new dependency, framework/language change, unrelated destructive action, or platform rejection stops the dependent action until the required scope/resource changes. The already approved x/mobile pin is not re-litigated.

Pause affects agent work, not the already deployed services. The existing30-minute cloud GPS schedule remains unchanged. No Windows scheduled automation was created or changed.

## map-access-contract - one cached PNG with two authorized retrieval paths

Outcome: serve the same real cached PNG to the signed-in owner app and to a keyed URL. Non-goals: dashboard adapters, anonymous S3 access, API-key access to commands/history, or a map read that polls the car.

- Planned endpoint: HTTPS GET /api/map.png. The Android app supplies its existing LWA-linked owner-session bearer handle; normal refresh/revocation checks apply.
- Planned embed URL pattern: https://suburban.jeremy.ninja/api/map.png?key=<MAP_READ_KEY> . The placeholder is documentation only. The real key must never enter chat, logs, Git, screenshots, command arguments or status email.
- A dedicated high-entropy map-read key authorizes only this PNG. It cannot start/unlock/lock, request a GPS wake, read raw history, mint owner sessions or administer the service. The app does not need this key.
- Validate the key in the Go handler with a constant-time digest comparison. Reject missing, duplicate, malformed, revoked or incorrect keys. No anonymous fallback and no redirect to an anonymously readable S3 URL.
- Key setup default: prepare the existing hidden local secret-entry flow for a map-only credential, retaining only its SHA256 digest as repository secret MAP_READ_KEY_SHA256 for deployment. The owner retains the raw key outside chat. Rotation replaces the digest and invalidates the previous URL after deployment; deleting/disabling the digest disables only keyed retrieval, not valid owner-session retrieval. No credential value is requested during planning.
- The keyed URL is a bearer credential: anyone holding it can retrieve the image. Restrict its use to the owner's private embed. Never put a real keyed URL in README, plan.md, logs or email. Dashboard-specific secret storage/proxying is deferred by the owner.
- Make S3 private: remove PublicCurrentMapOnly, enable all four public-access blocks, and grant the API role GetObject only on current.png. Worker PutObject remains scoped to current.png. Do not keep a public compatibility path.
- The API returns image/png bytes after authorization. Use private, no-store responses and no-referrer policy; exclude the key/query from access logs, error messages and telemetry. Any conditional/HEAD request must pass the same authorization first.
- Retrieval reads existing storage only. The cron worker alone owns 30-minute GPS polling and map regeneration when coordinates change. Preserve source observation time, last successful poll and map generation time as distinct facts; unknown source time stays unknown.
- GPS history stays encrypted with the existing 90-day expiry. No history endpoint or third-party tracking integration is added.

## next-release-manifest - intended 1.0.8 contents

### Not included

## Current job and restart policy

Image generation227/228 completed; both outputs were inspected and copied into the repository. Completed Android/backend/voice watchers28548/91358/53933 closed after successful terminal results. Each new final backend job receives an exact run ID and30-minute all-terminal watcher. Maximum three CI attempts per diagnosed fix; no unchanged deterministic rerun. Compilation/signing retries never authorize vehicle submissions. A lost/ambiguous action is never resent. S9 received one USB reconnect; later transport loss is input-blocked pending a stable connection, with no repeated resets. No Windows scheduled task, recurring helper or EC2 instance is created. Mobile's existing user-authorized4hour checks/reminders use the app lifecycle/framework OS APIs.

## Stop conditions (only these)

- Missing owner-only authentication/consent blocks that action only. The one-time linking secret-use exception is explicitly approved2026-10-24; no elapsed-time approval or automatic expansion applies.
- Unknown physical vehicle outcome or absent current owner readiness blocks vehicle mutations only. Continue synthetic/read-only/app work.
- A proven provider/framework limitation after the stated bounded attempts is recorded with evidence and exact external unblocker; do not invent an alternative auth path or authored non-Go glue.
- An unrelated destructive data change, material purchase, new external audience or a higher-priority platform rejection lacks authority. Finish independent authorized work and report the concrete requirement.

## Retained earlier plan and execution record

The following historical plan is retained verbatim except for explicit later corrections already recorded. Its source evidence and locked owner answers remain relevant; execute the current workstreams above for this completion run.

Status at original bootstrap: implementation started on 2026-10-22 with verify-report-envelope and readiness-preflight. Planning date: 2026-10-22. Repository: `C:\dev\Suburban-Remote`.

## Outcome and scope

Deliver Alexa, a directly installed Android APK, and web control of Jeremy's 2027 Chevrolet Suburban High Country Deluxe, with AWS data logging. All authored application code uses Go, including a port of the required OnStar protocol. OnStarJS2 is the port's source reference, not a Node.js runtime dependency. The web/API uses Go Fiber in Lambda, deployed with SAM by GitHub Actions on `main`. The finished service attempts vehicle-location collection every 15 minutes and exposes at most 90 days of observations; provider outages and stale fixes remain visible gaps.

This plan covers one owner and one vehicle unless Jeremy specifies otherwise. The vehicle's actual capabilities and active subscription determine available commands. No vehicle capability is inferred from a trim name. Public distribution, additional vehicles, billing, fleet management, and unsupported vehicle functions are outside this plan. Deliberately deferred features live in `C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\backlog.md`; a backlog item is never a plan item.

Implementation changes are tracked by the named milestones and Execution log below. Only checked items with recorded evidence are complete. Unchecked acceptance commands are requirements, not claims that the corresponding implementation or tests exist.

## Resolved decision register (answers recorded above; do not ask again)


These questions were answered by the dated implementation replies above. They remain here to preserve the exact options and their scope.

- `slot-wake-policy` — Option A: every 15-minute slot sends the digitaltwin `sms=true` wake request (fresh fix, 96 vehicle wake-ups per day, 12 V battery and throttling risk). Option B (recommended): each slot reads with `sms=false` and sends `sms=true` only when the cached fix is older than 15 minutes; owner-requested refresh always uses `sms=true`. Both options query OnStar every 15 minutes. Affects location-schedule.
- `four-hour-update-check` — Fyne v2.8.2 has no Android component that runs Go while the app is closed. Option A (recommended): check on every open and every four hours while foregrounded; on backgrounding, schedule a Fyne `App.ScheduleNotification` for now + 4 hours reading "Open Suburban Remote to check for updates"; the Go check runs at the next open. Option B: keep a persisted background network check as a hard requirement; mobile-contract is marked `[!]` until a Go-accessible background-execution path exists.
- `alexa-invocation-name` — default: `truck remote` (two words, lowercase, no trademark).
- `alexa-device-account` — If the Echo devices and Alexa app profile are not on the same Amazon account as the developer account that owns `ASK_VENDOR_ID`: Option A (recommended): re-register the devices to the developer account. Option B: create or move the skill under the developer account that already owns the devices. A beta test is not accepted, because it ends after at most 90 days.
- `failed-create-stack-delete` — Authorize CI to run `aws cloudformation delete-stack` on `suburban-remote` only when its status is `ROLLBACK_COMPLETE` (a failed first create holding no data)? Recommended: yes. Without it, `stack-repair` only reports that status and the run marks delivery `[!]`.
- `daily-digest` — Build the optional daily SES heartbeat digest in `operator-visibility`? Recommended: yes.
- `cost-center-tag` — `CostCenter=home-iot` (closest deploy.md bucket) unless Jeremy names another.
- `officepc-local-actions` — Permission, on OFFICEPC, for: (a) a temporary HKCU protocol-handler registration during GM enrollment; (b) attended `adb` capture over USB for handset-observation; (c) any fallback browser driving with `chromedp` if both manual enrollment inputs fail.

## Verified facts

- Local branch is `main`, clean at initial inspection. `git log -3 --format="%h %s"` returned `5acaf72 bootstrap: deploy.md (OIDC pipeline standard), CLAUDE.md, agents.md, set-secret script`.
- `git remote -v`: `https://github.com/JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote.git`. Existing tracked files are `deploy.md`, `agents.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `scripts/set-secret.sh`, and `scripts/set-secret.cmd`. No Go module, app, test suite, SAM template, or GitHub workflow exists.
- `deploy.md` records domain `Suburban.jeremy.ninja`, AWS account `759775734231`, and repository variables for role, domain, zone, certificates, and artifact bucket. These are repository declarations; live variable values have not yet been verified.
- Local hostname is `OfficePC`. Short commands must use hidden process launches, including all child processes. Browser/computer control needs the user's required permission. No local scheduled automation is permitted without an exact authorized schedule.
- `scripts/set-secret.sh` already supports hidden prompts, `--file`, and `--generate`. It sends values through stdin and reports only length, fingerprint, and update time. Do not replace this credential path gratuitously. `scripts/set-secret.cmd` is a thin wrapper that runs it through Git Bash.
- The local `gh` commands for repository metadata, variables, and secret names returned `To get started with GitHub CLI, please run:  gh auth login`. No secret values were requested or read. Check the GitHub connector before declaring repository access blocked.
- GitHub connector metadata verified repository ID `1429577133`, visibility `private`, default branch `main`, and push/admin access. Noninteractive `git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD` verified remote `main` at `5acaf7274144877e13bd0c1ecd1c48949866d11c`. Git delivery works; local `gh` credential-management access still needs setup before key installation.
- OnStarJS2 is an unofficial Node.js library, with authentication and vehicle support constraints. Its documentation instructs discovery through the account-vehicles response. Direct library use cannot also be a strictly Go-only runtime. [OnStarJS2](https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS)
- LWA refresh tokens can be long-lived but can be revoked. A five-year application retention policy cannot guarantee five years without reauthentication. [Amazon refresh tokens](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/login-with-amazon/refresh-token.html)
- ASK CLI generates LWA tokens for Alexa skill management; those are not end-user application sessions or GM credentials. [Amazon SMAPI tokens](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/smapi/get-access-token-smapi.html)
- DynamoDB TTL is asynchronous. Expired records must be excluded from reads while physical deletion is pending. [AWS TTL](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/ttl-expired-items.html)
- Fyne can package Go applications for Android and iOS. Android requires SDK/NDK tools; iOS requires macOS/Xcode. Native OS integration still needs a feasibility test. [Fyne packaging](https://docs.fyne.io/started/mobile/)
- Android periodic background work can be delayed by OS power management. A four-hour request is a target, not an exact execution guarantee while the app is closed. [Android periodic work](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/work/PeriodicWorkRequest)
- Review of the upstream 2.16.7 source found headed Chromium/Xvfb login, local token/profile files, and unguarded TOTP logging. These are migration hazards to avoid in the Go port, not code to copy blindly. [GM authentication source](https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS/blob/master/src/auth/GMAuth.ts)
- Upstream request code contains its own throttling retries and can return a location response still marked pending after its polling deadline. The Go port needs explicit completion/freshness checks and one retry budget. [Request source](https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS/blob/master/src/RequestService.ts)
- The common AWS Go/Fiber proxy imports Fiber v2 and is archived. Compatibility and maintenance must be checked together. [Adapter source](https://github.com/awslabs/aws-lambda-go-api-proxy/blob/master/fiber/adapter.go)

Verified by the plan editor in review round 2 on 2026-10-22. Sources were fetched that day into the session scratchpad (not committed); line numbers refer to those copies.

- `git ls-remote https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS refs/heads/master` returned `1ae0cbb6fd7601e871b67cd0cda02c80c4354f9d`. This is the candidate revision only; onstar-contract records the actual pin in the parity manifest and the readiness report.
- Upstream `RequestService.ts` (master): `location()` builds `{serviceUrl}/veh/datadelivery/digitaltwin/v1/vehicles/{VIN}` and requests it with `?sms=true|false&region=na` (lines 781–784), then polls until `telemetry.data.session.updatePending` is no longer `PENDING` (lines 795–798) inside `requestPollingTimeoutSeconds`; `requestPollingIntervalSeconds` defaults to 6 (line 79). `diagnostics()` is one GET to `/api/v1/vh/vehiclehealth/v1/healthstatus/{VIN}` (line 530). The file contains no `onStarPin` reference and sets no HTTP `timeout` option. [RequestService.ts](https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS/blob/master/src/RequestService.ts)
- Upstream `types.ts`: each health-status element carries `cts?: string | null; // ISO datetime` (line 259). Upstream defines no typed field paths for location coordinates, accuracy, or fix time.
- Upstream `GMAuth.ts` detects `<TITLE>Access Denied</TITLE>` in the login response (line 2311) and treats an Access Denied error as non-retryable (line 1521). [GMAuth.ts](https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS/blob/master/src/auth/GMAuth.ts)
- Fyne v2.8.2 ships exactly two Android Java files, `GoNativeActivity.java` and `FyneNotificationReceiver.java`. `GoNativeActivity` has no `onNewIntent` and calls `getIntent()` only to read component metadata (line 395). `FyneNotificationReceiver` sets no content intent. [GoNativeActivity v2.8.2](https://github.com/fyne-io/fyne/blob/v2.8.2/internal/driver/mobile/app/GoNativeActivity.java)
- Fyne v2.8.2 `CHANGELOG.md` line 76: "Adding accessibility support (currently off by default, use -tags accessibility to test)". [Fyne changelog](https://github.com/fyne-io/fyne/blob/develop/CHANGELOG.md)
- fyne tools (main): `AppOutputName` returns `.aab` for Android release builds (`cmd/fyne/internal/mobile/build.go` lines 45–48). `build_androidapp.go` reads a project `AndroidManifest.xml` as-is and renders its own template only when that file is absent (lines 67–83); its legacy asset path (no adaptive icon and no splash) does not apply `--app-build`/`--app-version` (lines 294–296). The tool's template uses `{{.Build}}`/`{{.Version}}` placeholders and declares `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, and `INTERNET`. Release signing code (`cmd/fyne/internal/commands/release.go`) calls `jarsigner`. [fyne tools](https://github.com/fyne-io/tools)
- `deploy.md` states that the `gha-deploy` role is org-wide and trusts `main`/`master`/`develop` branches, `v*` tags, and GitHub environments of the org's repositories.
- `deploy.md` records the domain as `Suburban.jeremy.ninja`. Plan convention: every registered LWA/Alexa callback, assetlinks or app-link host (none planned), and curl in this plan uses lowercase `suburban.jeremy.ninja`; the capitalized form names the same DNS record and is never used in a registered URL.

## Reviewer-cited facts (not yet re-verified)

A round-2 reviewer cited each fact below from the named primary source on 2026-10-22. The plan editor did not re-fetch these sources. They are assumptions: before a milestone relies on one, the `account-preconditions` item in readiness-preflight re-checks it and moves it to Verified facts with its source and date. Values that only CI can print (which certificate variable is the us-east-1 certificate, the lowercased `DOMAIN_NAME`, the deployed table key attributes) are recorded by `delivery-workflow / sam-skeleton`, not here.

- The archived adapter repository was archived 2025-05-21 and its go.mod pins `github.com/gofiber/fiber/v2 v2.52.5`. Fiber's current major is v3 (v3.6.1 latest release; v2.52.15 is the last v2 patch). [Fiber releases](https://github.com/gofiber/fiber/releases)
- Upstream package `onstarjs2@2.16.7`, MIT; the master commit is dated 2026-10-12. Upstream protocol changes so far: TOTP requirement (2024-11-19), 429 handling (2.10.0), v3 with v1 fallback (2.11.0), GraphQL `offers` field renames (2.16.0), Access Denied no-retry (2.16.6).
- Upstream login relies on patchright, headful Chromium, and randomized fingerprints, and its Access Denied message says to wait several hours or authenticate manually.
- fyne-io/fyne#2790 (Android intent support) and fyne-io/fyne#4953 (notification tap does not open the app) are open. `GoNativeActivity` launches only document-picker intents; `App.OpenURL` on Android always returns nil.
- fyne tools release builds target SDK 36 with a 16 KB linker flag and sign with `jarsigner` (APK Signature Scheme v1 only); non-release builds produce an `.apk` signed with an embedded debug key, target 29. `--app-build` maps to versionCode and defaults to 1 when ≤ 0. Android 11+ refuses v1-only APK installs. [Android 11 changes](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/11/behavior-changes-11)
- Android 15+ devices may use 16 KB pages; NDK r28+ aligns to 16 KB by default; check with `zipalign -c -P 16 -v 4`. [Page sizes](https://developer.android.com/guide/practices/page-sizes)
- Alexa PIN confirmation requires a custom skill with personalization enabled, and a user with a voice ID and profile PIN. If account linking is used, it must be to a specific person, not the device-registered Amazon account. PIN confirmation and Skills Personalization are turned on in the developer console under TOOLS > Permissions. [PIN confirmation](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/custom-skills/pin-confirmation-for-alexa-skills.html)
- A development-stage skill works only on devices registered to the developer account email (or a household/beta test); a beta test lasts at most 90 days and cannot be extended. [Testing](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/test/test-your-skill-overview.html)
- Skill events are managed only through the ASK CLI/SMAPI manifest (`SKILL_DISABLED`, `SKILL_ACCOUNT_LINKED`, `SKILL_ACCOUNT_UNLINKED`, …); freshness uses `eventPublishingTime` within 150 seconds. [Skill events](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/smapi/skill-events-in-alexa-skills.html)
- ASK CLI environment-credential mode reads `ASK_REFRESH_TOKEN` and `ASK_VENDOR_ID`; it has no custom client-secret variable. [ASK CLI credentials](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/smapi/manage-credentials-with-ask-cli.html)
- A Lambda Alexa trigger without a skill ID overrides triggers with one; the manifest endpoint is the Lambda ARN. [Host a skill on Lambda](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/custom-skills/host-a-custom-skill-as-an-aws-lambda-function.html)
- GitHub Actions `permissions:` has no key for repository Actions secrets; deleting a secret through REST needs a collaborator token with `repo` scope. [Workflow syntax](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/writing-workflows/workflow-syntax-for-github-actions), [Secrets REST](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/secrets)
- EventBridge Scheduler needs an execution role trusted by `scheduler.amazonaws.com`; a FIFO target needs `SqsParameters.MessageGroupId`; a schedule DLQ cannot be FIFO. An SQS FIFO source needs a FIFO DLQ. [Scheduler setup](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/scheduler/latest/UserGuide/setting-up.html), [SqsParameters](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/scheduler/latest/APIReference/API_SqsParameters.html)
- Lambda SQS mapping: function timeout must be ≤ visibility timeout; AWS recommends visibility ≥ 6 × function timeout; a FIFO group delivers no further message until the in-flight one is deleted or visible again; `ScalingConfig.MaximumConcurrency` accepts 2–1,000. [SQS configure](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/services-sqs-configure.html), [SQS scaling](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/services-sqs-scaling.html)
- With the default service key, any principal with `lambda:GetFunctionConfiguration` reads Lambda environment variables in plaintext.
- SAM `DynamoDBCrudPolicy` grants `dynamodb:Scan`. [SAM policy templates](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/serverless-application-model/latest/developerguide/serverless-policy-template-list.html)
- HTTP API payload 2.0 has no multi-value fields: duplicate headers and query keys are comma-joined, request cookies arrive in `cookies`, and response cookies must be returned in `cookies`. [HTTP API Lambda integration](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/apigateway/latest/developerguide/http-api-develop-integrations-lambda.html)

## Preconditions and explicit assumptions

All four asked decisions are locked above. No permission to weaken the Go-only requirement, omit an app, or perform unattended actuation is inferred. The open decisions above are answered before the unattended run.

Assumptions: personal single-owner use; `us-east-1`; no new subscription purchase; no firm deadline; location logging applies only to the owner's enrolled vehicle. Logical history ends at 90 days even if DynamoDB's physical deletion runs later. Four-hour mobile checks honor Android scheduling limits and the `four-hour-update-check` answer. These platform limits are stated acceptance boundaries, not guarantees of exact deletion or execution time.

Required attended preflight before an unattended implementation run: confirm the actual handset/Android version and package identity; confirm OnStar remote-service entitlement and access to the vehicle account privately; Jeremy confirms privately that the vehicle shows in the myChevrolet garage with OnStar active and that the account is the owner, not a shared user (record presence/status only); verify GitHub secret-write access; identify an existing owner-controlled signing-key recovery destination; confirm the Echo/Alexa app account matches the developer account. Complete Amazon and GM consent in their named milestones after the required helper/routes exist. None of these private values go in this plan. Preflight reports presence/status only. Missing credentials block dependent live proofs, while source/fixture work can proceed. Supervised vehicle tests remain an attended final gate, so a run cannot claim full completion without them.

## Architecture decisions (reviewed, not user-locked)

These came from review round 2. They are plan decisions, not Locked decisions; Jeremy may override them, but the run does not reopen them on its own.

- HTTP ingress uses an owned in-repo adapter `internal/lambdahttp` and the latest tagged Fiber v3 release at implementation start; the archived `aws-lambda-go-api-proxy` is a translation reference only.
- Two Go modules: root (Lambdas, domain, helpers) and `mobile/` (Fyne). The root module never imports Fyne.
- One FIFO queue with two message groups per vehicle (`cmd-<vehicleKey>`, `loc-<vehicleKey>`) plus a State-table GM lease with a fencing token.
- Credential encryption uses two fixed key slots (`A`/`B`) and an active-slot variable, with re-encrypt-on-read.
- Web/mobile and Alexa use one shared LWA security profile, as locked by the user on 2026-10-22.
- Mobile login is browser-out/poll-back; Android App Links are not used.
- Android updates install through the system browser and OS installer; the app does not declare `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES`.

## Proposed architecture

Two Go modules. The root module `github.com/JeremyProffittOrg/suburban-remote` holds the Lambdas, domain, store, provider, helper commands, and `pkg/contract` (release-manifest schema, API DTOs, Ed25519 verification). The module `github.com/JeremyProffittOrg/suburban-remote/mobile` under `mobile/` holds the Fyne app, the mobile UI packages, and the update client, and imports `pkg/contract`. The root module never imports Fyne. A root `go.work` is for local development only; CI Lambda/SAM builds and root tests run with `GOWORK=off`. Both go.mod files pin `go` and `toolchain`; `actions/setup-go` uses `go-version-file` pointing at each module's go.mod. Keep domain logic independent of Fiber. Start with these concrete entrypoints, adding only files required by each milestone:

- `cmd/api`: Go Fiber web pages, LWA callbacks, mobile API, command-status API, permission manifest, and release metadata. HTTPS through API Gateway and the existing domain/certificate.
- `cmd/worker`: Go SQS consumer for vehicle operations, including scheduled location reads, and consumer of the scheduler dead-letter queue. The Go OnStar protocol implementation is private to this worker.
- EventBridge Scheduler sends location requests directly to the FIFO queue. There is no extra poller Lambda or second provider-access path.
- `cmd/alexa`: Go Alexa request handler using the same command authorization and status model. A custom skill covers vehicle-specific intents.
- `cmd/notify`: Go Lambda that consumes a standard notification queue and sends owner email through SES v2 (security events; daily digest only if `daily-digest` is approved). It never calls GM.
- `mobile/cmd/mobile` (in the `mobile/` module): Go mobile UI, provisionally Fyne. Verify secure device storage, browser login return, permission prompts, update checks, TalkBack support, and browser-mediated installation using Go APIs or existing framework support before committing to it. A passing APK build alone is insufficient.
- Helper entrypoints (root module, never deployed as Lambdas): `cmd/verify` (acceptance checks), `cmd/onstar-enroll` (owner-run GM enrollment and reauthentication), `cmd/secretctl` (the Go helper behind `scripts/secretctl.bat` for hidden entry, key generation, ASK token capture, LWA owner-ID lookup, and stdin secret writes), `cmd/paramwriter` (the CI-only stdin parameter streamer used in private-parameter-delivery), and `cmd/androidmanifest` (renders the Android manifest template with literal version values before each `fyne package`; it uses only `text/template`, never Fyne).
- SAM owns the HTTP API, Lambda functions, queues and dead-letter queues, schedule, DynamoDB tables, log groups, IAM roles, and release bucket. GitHub Actions owns build, verification, signing, deployment, and release publication.

Packages: `internal/onstar`, `internal/commands`, `internal/vehicle`, `internal/location`, `internal/auth`, `internal/permissions`, `internal/web`, `internal/alexa`, `internal/lambdahttp`, `internal/worker`, `internal/state`, `internal/logging`, `internal/notify`, `internal/verify`, `internal/failpoint`, `internal/uitest`, `internal/testdata`, and in the mobile module `internal/mobile` and `internal/update`. Do not add an `internal/provider` interface or a generic provider framework. Domain packages take a time source and a narrow store interface defined where it is used. Tests use an in-memory store and a fixed clock; conditional-write behavior is also tested against DynamoDB Local (see Vehicle commands). `internal/onstar` is tested against an `httptest` server that replays the source-derived fixtures, with injectable failure points for the crash points listed in Vehicle commands.

Use `provided.al2023`, arm64, and `GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm64 CGO_ENABLED=0` for Go Lambdas. HTTP ingress is API Gateway HTTP API, payload format 2.0. `internal/lambdahttp` converts `events.APIGatewayV2HTTPRequest` into a fasthttp request, runs Fiber's `app.Handler()`, and converts the result into `events.APIGatewayV2HTTPResponse`. The archived `awslabs/aws-lambda-go-api-proxy` (archived 2025-05-21; imports Fiber v2 only) is a translation reference only. Its actual license is Apache-2.0 (verified during implementation); record reference provenance in `internal/lambdahttp` and do not import it. The owned adapter is authored independently. Pin the exact Fiber v3 release that is the latest tagged release when implementation starts, and record its version and checksum. The adapter builds the URL from `RawPath` and `RawQueryString`, never from `QueryStringParameters`. It joins the `cookies` array into one `Cookie` header. It copies response headers into single values, except `Set-Cookie`, whose values go into `Cookies`. It sets `IsBase64Encoded` for non-UTF-8 bodies, defaults the status to 200, and propagates the Lambda `context.Context`: before `app.Handler()` runs it sets `fctx.SetUserValue(lambdaCtxKey, ctx)`, and the first middleware reads that value and calls Fiber v3 `c.SetContext(ctx)` (v2 named this `SetUserContext`). Every DynamoDB, LWA, and SQS call in `internal/web` uses `c.Context()`. Handlers derive `context.WithTimeout(c.Context(), 8*time.Second)`, and middleware maps `context.DeadlineExceeded` to HTTP 504, so neither the Lambda timeout nor the HTTP API 30-second integration limit fires first.

TestLambdaHTTPContract uses API Gateway HTTP API payload 2.0 JSON fixtures under `internal/web/testdata/` and checks what the handler and the client actually receive through `internal/lambdahttp`. It asserts: (1) for rawQueryString `a=1&a=2`, the handler sees `1` and `2` as separate values, and `b=x%2Cy` reaches the handler as one value; (2) a request `cookies` array with two entries reaches the handler as two cookies; (3) a handler that sets two cookies produces a response with both in `cookies`; (4) repeated non-cookie response headers keep all values; (5) a binary body sets `isBase64Encoded: true`, and a PNG returns byte-for-byte through the deployed API; (6) redirects keep status and `Location`; (7) the request context exposes sourceIp and requestId for logging without trusting a client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For`; (8) 404/405 defaults apply.

The website uses Go HTML templates, HTML forms, CSS, and server-rendered status pages; no authored JavaScript or service worker is required.

Lambda runtime configuration (SAM template constants, checked by `go run ./cmd/verify template`):

- `cmd/worker`: `Timeout: 120`, `MemorySize: 256`. The handler derives its provider-operation context from the Lambda context deadline minus a 20-second persistence/acknowledgement reserve (effective operation deadline ≤ 100 seconds), never from a fixed constant; the 90-second polling deadline fits inside it. Event source mapping: `BatchSize: 1`, no batching window, `ScalingConfig.MaximumConcurrency: 2`, no reserved concurrency on the worker.
- `cmd/api`: `Timeout: 10`, `MemorySize: 256`. `cmd/alexa`: `Timeout: 7` (five-second internal deadline plus margin), `MemorySize: 256`. `cmd/notify`: `Timeout: 30`, `MemorySize: 128`.
- `cmd/verify template` fails if `VisibilityTimeout` < 6 × worker `Timeout`, or worker `Timeout` − 20 < the 90-second polling deadline.

Each Go Lambda does all one-time work once, before or at its first invocation, through package-level initialization or a `sync.Once` reached from `main` before `lambda.Start`: load AWS SDK v2 config and build DynamoDB/SQS/S3/SES clients with one shared `http.Client` (keep-alive); decode encryption keys where the function receives them; for the API function only, parse `html/template` sets from an `embed.FS`, compute fingerprinted names from the embedded `static/` tree, and build the Fiber app plus adapter. Handlers construct nothing per request. Provisioned concurrency is not used.

Runtime IAM: one role per Lambda function, inline statements only, explicit actions on explicit resource ARNs. Do not use SAM policy templates (for example, `DynamoDBCrudPolicy` grants `dynamodb:Scan`) or `*` actions. No role is granted `dynamodb:Scan` or `logs:CreateLogGroup`; only the worker role gets SQS receive/delete/visibility actions. Grant each function only the table, queue, and bucket actions its code calls, and record that list next to the function in `template.yaml`. Runtime execution roles have no `lambda:Get*` permission on each other.

Queue trust: the FIFO queue has an explicit `AWS::SQS::QueuePolicy` allowing `sqs:SendMessage` only from the Scheduler execution role and the `cmd/api`/`cmd/alexa` execution roles. SAM's `SchedulerExecutionRole` is trusted only by `scheduler.amazonaws.com` with `aws:SourceAccount` = `759775734231` and `aws:SourceArn` built by `!Sub` for the exact `schedule-group/default` ARN (AWS Scheduler requires a schedule-group ARN, not a schedule ARN; verified during implementation). Its only permissions are `sqs:SendMessage` on the vehicle FIFO queue and on the scheduler DLQ. The worker treats every message body as a pointer: it re-reads the durable command/slot record, applies the conditional `dispatching` transition and the owner/grant recheck, and accepts a location message only if its schedule ARN equals the configured schedule and its scheduled time falls on a 15-minute slot boundary inside the five-minute window. Any other location message is acknowledged and recorded as `stale` without a provider call.

No VPC/NAT, always-on host, EC2, paid monitoring, or new key-management service is planned. Reuse existing ACM certificates for HTTPS; TLS certificate private keys do not need generation or export to GitHub.

## Go protocol port and readiness limits

Pin the exact OnStarJS2 source commit/package checksum and retain MIT attribution for translated code. Translate the required authentication, discovery, commands, diagnostics, and status polling into `internal/onstar`; do not create a generic provider framework. Map protocol calls and response semantics to the source revision in a parity manifest. Use synthetic, redacted request/response fixtures; no live credential or location capture in fixtures. Fixtures use only the synthetic VIN, account and vehicle IDs, and coordinate constants defined in `internal/testdata/synthetic.go`.

Use one owner-run Go enrollment/reauthentication helper for the source authorization-code/PKCE flow, followed by Go net/http token refresh, GM token exchange, and vehicle requests in Lambda. The server ships neither Node nor Chromium. The attended helper generates state and a PKCE S256 verifier, opens the published authorization URL in a fresh visible Playwright Chromium session, and waits up to ten minutes. The owner enters GM credentials and MFA directly in the browser; the helper neither fills nor reads those fields. Playwright response interception captures the exact registered msauth.com.gm.myChevrolet://auth callback in memory, validates its state, and suppresses external protocol launch before passing it to the existing Go exchange. There is no manual Network-panel copying or registry handler. Browser diagnostics, screenshots, traces, HAR, and exported state are disabled. No fingerprint evasion or automatic full reauthentication is allowed. Browser setup precedes spending an attempt; capture denials use the existing persisted cooldown. The explicit 2026-10-22 Playwright authorization supersedes the earlier manual-only path. Provider acceptance remains a live feasibility gate.

Reproduce token scopes/audiences, expiry units, stable device identity, and refresh persistence with explicit deadlines. Preserve the existing refresh token if the response omits a replacement; conditionally persist a replacement before subsequent work. Two separate Lambda cold starts, a real refresh, and a read-only vehicle/location request must pass. Normal deployment never reseeds rotated credentials. A revoked GM grant produces `reauth-required`, pauses provider work, and leaves the independent LWA session intact. Unofficial GM access may require later human reauthentication; five-year LWA retention cannot change that. If normal enrollment or refresh fails the bounded proof, stop GM-dependent work rather than add a second authentication path. Retain both upstream license notices. [Original license](https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS/blob/master/LICENSE-Original), [fork license](https://github.com/BigThunderSR/OnStarJS/blob/master/LICENSE)

After the GM token exchange, decode the GM access token claims without signature verification, as upstream does, and require a `vehs` entry that matches the enrolled VIN. A missing `vehs` claim, or an absent VIN, is the persisted state `vehicle-not-linked`. That state pauses provider work and keeps the stored credential. Do not port upstream's delete-and-re-authenticate branch: never rename, delete, or re-request tokens on this condition, and never count it against the attended enrollment attempts. Web, mobile, and Alexa report the state and name the myChevrolet garage as the fix location.

Likewise, Go-only Android must prove OS-backed secure storage, browser-out/poll-back login, permission handling, browser-mediated installer handoff, TalkBack, and the update-check path chosen by `four-hour-update-check`. If Fyne or its existing Go-accessible dependencies cannot provide a required capability without authored non-Go code, record a failed feasibility result before product construction. Do not replace the Android app with a website, pretend a foreground timer is a background worker, or author native code through a code-generation loophole.

The mobile reviewer found no verified maintained package covering the full contract. Fyne's scheduled notification receiver does not perform a network version check. Its newer secure-preferences work is version-dependent and has fallback behavior that must not store these handles outside the accepted Android security path. This is a feasibility gap, not proof that Go cannot implement it. Start with one pinned Fyne proof (2.8.0 or later). A proof build is a release-signed APK that passes local and CI build checks (compile, lint, `apksigner verify`), is installed on the target handset, and is run through the full `go run ./cmd/verify android-readiness --report artifacts/android-readiness.json` evidence set. Compile, lint, packaging, and signing iterations before installation do not count toward the proof-build ceiling; each such failure is recorded in the Execution log, and toolchain iterations are capped at the CI delivery ceiling of three attempts per fix. If the proof fails after three proof builds total, retain the blocker and continue independent backend/web/Alexa work. Do not reopen the locked language choice. [Fyne notification source](https://github.com/fyne-io/fyne/blob/v2.8.2/internal/driver/mobile/app/FyneNotificationReceiver.java), [secure preferences source](https://github.com/fyne-io/fyne/blob/d51966952ab7d31936b822cba7d8c0427777cb7e/app/secret_preferences_android.go)

## Identity, keys, and permissions

Use separate trust boundaries for LWA user sign-in, ASK/SMAPI deployment authority, and GM/OnStar access. An operator-controlled bootstrap binds the server-verified Amazon subject to the enrolled vehicle; the first public login cannot claim ownership. Do not use email matching or client-submitted profile data. Request `profile:user_id`, validate the token audience against the registered client ID, and retrieve the subject from Amazon. This scope has no Amazon consent screen, so the app must separately explain and record control/location consent. [Amazon profile scope](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/login-with-amazon/customer-profile.html), [token validation](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/login-with-amazon/obtain-customer-profile.html)

Owner bootstrap mechanism: the owner's Amazon subject is the GitHub repository variable `OWNER_AMAZON_USER_ID` (an `amzn1.account.…` identifier; not a secret, per deploy.md's variable rule). It is a plain SAM parameter and reaches `cmd/api`, `cmd/worker`, and `cmd/alexa` as the environment variable `OWNER_AMAZON_USER_ID`. `cmd/api` compares it with the `user_id` that the LWA profile endpoint returns for an audience-validated token. `cmd/alexa` makes the same comparison with the profile `user_id` resolved from the linked-account access token, never with the request's skill-scoped userId or personId. On a mismatch the response is `not-owner`: no session, no device grant, no Alexa binding; the log records only a hashed subject. The State table Owner item is created conditionally on the first successful match and records `boundAt` and the matched value; no other path creates it. Before each dispatch `cmd/worker` checks that the Owner item still matches `OWNER_AMAZON_USER_ID` and fails closed if not. To change the owner, change the variable and deploy; the deploy invalidates existing grants whose subject differs, and Git and Actions keep the audit trail. The value is obtained during amazon-enrollment: `cmd/secretctl owner-id` performs one LWA login against the registered security profile and prints only the `user_id`, never tokens. The operator then runs `gh variable set OWNER_AMAZON_USER_ID --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --body <value>`.

Use authorization-code login, PKCE S256, and one-use state. The Go backend exchanges the LWA code. Reserve `https://suburban.jeremy.ninja/auth/lwa/callback` and `https://suburban.jeremy.ninja/privacy`; these are planned routes, not currently deployed pages. Verify domain/certificate and register the exact delivered URLs before collecting provider credentials. Record console-supplied regional Alexa callbacks rather than guessing them. [LWA code flow](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/login-with-amazon/authorization-code-grant.html)

LWA security profile: web/mobile and Alexa account linking use the same profile and GitHub variable `LWA_CLIENT_ID`. Register `https://suburban.jeremy.ninja/auth/lwa/callback` and the console-supplied regional Alexa return URLs on that profile. Account-linking values: Authorization URI `https://www.amazon.com/ap/oa`; Access Token URI `https://api.amazon.com/auth/o2/token`; type `AUTH_CODE`; scope `profile:user_id`; access-token scheme `HTTP_BASIC`; `skipOnEnablement: false`. Alexa, not the skill Lambda, performs code exchange and refresh. The operator enters the same profile client ID and `LWA_CLIENT_SECRET` locally in the Alexa developer console account-linking page. The secret is also stored in GitHub for API/worker use, never in the skill Lambda, ASK deployment job, or agent-visible output; CI never calls `update-account-linking-info` with a secret. `cmd/api`, `cmd/worker`, and `cmd/alexa` accept only `aud == LWA_CLIENT_ID`, validated through LWA tokeninfo, then compare the profile `user_id` with the bound owner under the same fresh-versus-cached rules as other clients. amazon-enrollment proves the owner once against this shared profile. Rotate the shared secret through the hidden GitHub credential helper and deploy, then update the Alexa account-linking console to the same secret; record both consumers in operator-handoff.

Mobile login opens the system browser through Fyne `App.OpenURL`. The app generates a 32-byte verifier with `crypto/rand`, POSTs its SHA-256 to `/auth/mobile/start`, and receives a one-use session code valid for 10 minutes. The browser completes LWA at `/auth/mobile/begin?code=...`. The app polls `/auth/mobile/poll` every 3 seconds, with a 10-minute deadline, and sends the verifier. The server returns the device handle exactly once, only after LWA success, and only to the matching verifier; then it invalidates the code. Rate-limit start and poll per device/IP, and fail closed on excess. Test interception (the code alone is useless), wrong verifier, replay, cancellation, cold launch (the verifier stays in Android-backed secure storage until the poll completes or expires), and two simultaneous attempts. No tokens in URLs. No embedded WebView. Android App Links (`/auth/mobile/complete`, `/.well-known/assetlinks.json`) are not used: Fyne v2.8.2 GoNativeActivity has no `onNewIntent` and does not pass incoming intent data to Go (fyne-io/fyne#2790 is open). The app-link route is recorded in `backlog.md`. [Native OAuth requirements](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8252)

Web sessions use Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite cookies and CSRF protection on writes; no provider tokens in browser storage. Mobile stores only its own revocable handle in Android-backed secure storage. Keep provider secrets server-side. App access sessions last at most 15 minutes. High-impact commands and every scheduled location dispatch require fresh LWA authority validation; neither may use the validation cache. Other app reads may use validation at most 15 minutes old. Fail closed on unverifiable authority. Revocation is discovered at the next active check, not magically while idle. Retain the revocable credential family for at most five calendar years from the original grant; rotation preserves that fixed deadline. Never create a five-year bearer access token.

Session model: after the owner binding exists, a successful LWA login issues a long-lived opaque refresh handle and a 15-minute opaque access session. On web the handle is a `__Host-` prefixed Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite cookie; on mobile it is the value held in Android-backed secure storage. The handle is 32 random bytes from Go `crypto/rand`; the server stores only its SHA-256 hash, with device label, creation time, last-use time, and family ID. Every use rotates the handle, with a short grace window for the tested refresh race. Presenting a superseded handle outside that window revokes the whole family and forces login again. Idle expiry is 30 days; the absolute limit is the five-calendar-year family deadline. The web settings page lists every device family with its last-use time and can revoke any one family (PIN-confirmed) or all of them through global disconnect. Revocation applies at the next access-session mint and at the next dispatch check, never later than 15 minutes.

Device logout revokes that device's handles. Global disconnect revokes all devices and Alexa bindings, stops polling, rejects queued work, and deletes provider token material. Unlinking Alexa revokes Alexa access only. Every worker rechecks the active owner/grant/consent immediately before dispatch. Five-year expiry is enforced in reads and dispatch, independent of delayed TTL deletion. These rules cannot cancel an action already accepted by GM.

Generated keys are created once in a non-logging process and installed in `JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote` GitHub secrets without exposing values. Inventory names/status before creating anything; reuse `set-secret.sh` for supported modes. Use a real `.bat` entry point (`scripts/secretctl.bat`, distinct from the existing `scripts/set-secret.cmd` wrapper) with the Go helper `cmd/secretctl` for safe Windows credential entry/capture and key generation; preserve empty-input skip behavior and stdin secret writes. Signing tools may be dependencies, but project helpers remain Go. A 48-byte generic secret from the existing helper is not automatically an AES-256 or Ed25519 key. Provider-issued Amazon/GM credentials require their actual authorization flows.

Names-only inventory to finalize before collection:

- `LWA_CLIENT_SECRET`: Amazon-issued for the shared web/mobile/Alexa profile; consumers are API, the worker's owner-grant refresh/validation, and Alexa's account-linking service. The operator enters the same secret locally in the Alexa account-linking console. Never pass it to the skill Lambda or ASK deployment job. The skill Lambda validates its linked person token without holding management credentials or this secret. `LWA_CLIENT_ID` is the sole client-ID GitHub variable.
- `ASK_REFRESH_TOKEN` (secret) and `ASK_VENDOR_ID` (variable): issued during attended amazon-enrollment by `ask util generate-lwa-tokens --no-browser`; the vendor ID comes from `ask smapi get-vendor-list` in the same attended session. `ALEXA_SKILL_ID` is a variable. The skill deployment job uses only the pinned ASK CLI in environment-credential mode, sets `ASK_REFRESH_TOKEN` and `ASK_VENDOR_ID` in that step's environment only, and must complete with no `~/.ask` profile file and no prompt. Default to the CLI's built-in LWA client, so no `ASK_CLIENT_ID` or `ASK_CLIENT_SECRET` exists. Add `ASK_CLIENT_ID` (variable) and `ASK_CLIENT_SECRET` (secret) only if Go code calls SMAPI directly or the developer account cannot use the built-in client; in either case prove in CI that refresh works with that client before relying on it. Never deploy ASK management tokens into a Lambda.
- `ONSTAR_TOKEN_BOOTSTRAP`: one versioned JSON document, defined by a single Go type shared by the enrollment helper and the CI import step: `{"schema":1,"enrollment_version":<n>,"vin":"<enrolled VIN>","refresh_token":"<B2C refresh token>","refresh_obtained_at":<epoch s>,"b2c_subject":"<sub claim from the id_token>"}`, plus `"onstar_pin"` only if the proven command protocol needs the OnStar PIN (upstream never transmits it). It contains no password, TOTP secret, B2C access token, id_token, or GM API token. The worker gets the GM API token by token exchange on first use with the `device_id` from `ONSTAR_DEVICE_ID`; enrollment must use that same device ID, and the import fails if the bootstrap was made with a different one. The runtime State item stores these fields plus current B2C and GM access tokens and their `expires_at` values, encrypted as below. The PIN, if present, is never a separate GitHub secret, SAM parameter, or Lambda environment variable. onstar-contract decides which identity claim later refreshes are checked against (`sub` or upstream's uid/email comparison), and its fixtures prove that choice. `ONSTAR_DEVICE_ID` is generated once and protected as installation identity. GM password/MFA are entered locally into the provider flow and are not retained. VIN and account identifiers are supplied privately and excluded from logs.
- `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY_A` and `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY_B`: two fixed key slots, each 32 random bytes from Go `crypto/rand`, base64-encoded. API/worker only (and the CI seed step), used for nothing else (not sessions, not handoff codes). The repository variable `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY_ACTIVE` (`A` or `B`) names the slot for new encryptions. Both slots reach only the API and worker Lambdas as NoEcho parameters. Each encrypted item stores `keySlot` plus a monotonically increasing `keyGeneration`; AAD is `owner|provider|keySlot|keyGeneration|itemKey`. Decrypt selects the key by `keySlot` and fails closed on authentication failure with `reauth-required` reason `key-mismatch`, which is separate from provider revocation and does not consume GM enrollment attempts until the key mismatch is ruled out. A successful decrypt under the inactive slot immediately re-encrypts under the active slot with a conditional write on the token version. Rotation procedure (attended-free; never reads any value back): (1) `cmd/secretctl` writes a fresh random value into the inactive slot only, then flip `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY_ACTIVE` and push; (2) after the next scheduled poll, run `go run ./cmd/verify credentials --mode deployed --expect-key-slot <active>`, which reports only counts per slot and exits 0 only with zero items on the inactive slot; (3) only then is that slot eligible for overwrite at the next rotation. Rotation never requires GM or LWA re-enrollment, and the repo builds no rotation service. The org `credential-rotation` manifest lists these names as `kind: manual` (or excluded) with a pointer to the runbook; that change is made in the org repo, not this one.
- `ANDROID_SIGNING_KEYSTORE_B64`, `ANDROID_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`, `ANDROID_KEY_PASSWORD`: Android release job only; fixed alias (`ANDROID_KEY_ALIAS`) and package ID are variables. Verify the keystore size fits GitHub's secret limit before choosing its encoding.
- `UPDATE_MANIFEST_SIGNING_KEY`: generated Ed25519 private key, release job only; public verification key ships in the app. The private signing key never ships in the APK or website.
- Non-secret variables added in round 2: `OWNER_AMAZON_USER_ID`, `LWA_CLIENT_ID`, `ASK_VENDOR_ID`, `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY_ACTIVE`, `ENROLLMENT_VERSION`, `OWNER_NOTIFY_EMAIL`, `NOTIFY_FROM` (an SES-verified `@jeremy.ninja` sender).

GM bootstrap is a one-use CI import, not a SAM parameter or Lambda environment variable. A Go step receives the bootstrap and the active encryption key through the step environment, encrypts in memory, and conditionally seeds the State item with an enrollment version. Verify successful import without reading values. `GITHUB_TOKEN` has no permission for repository Actions secrets. CI never deletes secrets, and no PAT, fine-grained token, or GitHub App credential is created for that purpose. Cleanup is attended: immediately after the CI import run succeeds, Jeremy runs `gh secret delete ONSTAR_TOKEN_BOOTSTRAP --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote`. Enrollment is complete only when `gh secret list --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --json name --jq '.[].name' | grep -c '^ONSTAR_TOKEN_BOOTSTRAP$'` prints 0. The seed step in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` passes `${{ secrets.ONSTAR_TOKEN_BOOTSTRAP }}` through the step `env` (secrets cannot be used in `if:`). When the value is empty, the Go seed step exits 0, logs `seed: skipped, no bootstrap present`, and performs zero writes. A non-empty value is rejected when the State item already holds an enrollment version, unless the repository variable `ENROLLMENT_VERSION` is higher than the stored version. Failed import retains recoverable input without logging it. Ordinary deploys never import or resurrect credentials. Explicit relink gets a new enrollment version and the same cleanup rule.

Generate the Android release certificate with at least 25 years' validity. Keep a restricted owner-controlled recovery copy outside GitHub and test that it signs a fixture with the same public certificate; GitHub secrets cannot be downloaded as a backup. Inventory issuer, consumer, storage, injection, recovery owner, and revocation for each secret. Existing ACM supplies HTTPS; opaque server-side sessions need no extra web signing key. [Android signing](https://developer.android.com/studio/publish/app-signing)

Use the requested `ask util generate-lwa-tokens --no-browser` for ASK management authorization. The pinned CLI's normal token output must be intercepted by `cmd/secretctl` and sent directly to secret stdin; do not run it in a transcript-captured agent terminal. First prove hidden prompts, output capture, and failure cleanup with fake credentials. Display only the authorization URL and safe status/length. The user authorizes locally. No credential in argv, chat, temporary files, or debug logs. Verify installed secret names/update status, not values. [ASK CLI](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/smapi/ask-cli-command-reference.html)

Store changeable provider tokens in a restricted DynamoDB credential item using AES-256-GCM, a new random nonce per encryption, and the associated data above. The generated keys reach only required Lambda environments. Use conditional token-version updates and a short refresh lease so concurrent requests cannot overwrite a newer token. Keep the current token record authoritative; a later deployment must not replay an old GitHub bootstrap token. Use DynamoDB AWS-owned encryption, S3 service encryption, SQS-managed encryption, and Lambda default service encryption without provisioning custom KMS keys. Redact values before serialization. Secrets, authentication codes, installation IDs, VINs, coordinates, raw provider bodies, and authorization URLs containing tokens stay out of logs and artifacts.

Each application gets a versioned permission manifest listing capability, purpose, required/optional state, grant source, and denied behavior. On opening, inspect permissions and request missing grants that the platform permits. Browser/OS rules may require a user gesture; do not claim automatic OS prompts for manifest-only permissions. Show denied state and a Settings path. Core vehicle location is read from OnStar, so phone GPS permission is not inherently required. Do not request microphone, contacts, phone GPS, or broad storage without an implemented feature that needs them. Alexa account linking and permission cards follow Amazon's grant model.

Manifest deliverables are `permissions/web.json`, `permissions/mobile.json`, and `permissions/alexa.json`, plus the actual Android manifest and Alexa skill manifest. Cross-check the merged APK manifest against the declared list. Android `INTERNET` has no runtime prompt; `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` is optional on supported versions. The app does not declare `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES`; installation goes through the system browser and the OS installer, and the trusted-source prompt belongs to the browser. Declare any framework scheduler components/permissions found necessary by the proof. Denial must preserve a prompt on next open. Web Permissions-Policy denies unused device features; application consent covers owner control and background vehicle tracking. The Android manifest source is `mobile/cmd/mobile/AndroidManifest.xml.tmpl`: declarative configuration, not authored glue, exempted by path (together with its rendered output) from the mobile-evidence audit. The fyne tool uses a project `AndroidManifest.xml` as-is, renders no placeholders in it, and on its legacy asset path does not apply `--app-build`/`--app-version` (Verified facts). Therefore, before each `fyne package`, CI runs `go run ./cmd/androidmanifest --template mobile/cmd/mobile/AndroidManifest.xml.tmpl --version-code N --version-name 1.0.N --out mobile/cmd/mobile/AndroidManifest.xml`, which renders literal package, versionCode, and versionName values with Go `text/template`. The rendered file is git-ignored and never committed. The `.tmpl` is copied from the pinned fyne tool's AndroidManifest.xml template at the pinned SHA. It keeps the `lib_name` meta-data, `GoNativeActivity`, and `FyneNotificationReceiver`, sets `android:debuggable="false"`, takes package, versionCode, and versionName only from `cmd/androidmanifest`, removes `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` and `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, and adds only permissions that `permissions/mobile.json` lists (such as `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` and any scheduler permission the proof shows is necessary). The fyne tool sets targetSdkVersion; the value comes from the built APK and is recorded. [Android notifications](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/notification-permission)

## Threat model (accepted risks)

- Lambda environment variables use the default service key, so any principal with `lambda:GetFunctionConfiguration` or `lambda:GetFunction` on the API or worker function can read them in plaintext. This includes `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY_A`/`_B` and `LWA_CLIENT_SECRET`. These principals include account administrators and the org-wide `gha-deploy` role, which the `main`/`master`/`develop` branches of every JeremyProffittOrg repository can assume (deploy.md). That role can also redeploy this stack, so readable environment variables add little to its existing power. Accepted for single-owner personal use, because a custom KMS key is excluded by a locked decision. Mitigations in this plan: the Alexa function receives neither value; runtime execution roles have no `lambda:Get*` permission on each other; only the roles that decrypt provider tokens can read the State credential item.
- Narrowing `gha-deploy` trust to this repository is a change in the `credential-rotation` repository and is recorded in `backlog.md`, not in this plan.
- Under the explicit no-PIN decision, an unlocked authorized phone can issue supported vehicle commands. Owner-bound sessions, fresh authority checks, revocation, and control flags remain the authorization boundary.

## Vehicle commands and failure semantics

Build a capability matrix from the pinned library source, actual account/vehicle responses, and the controls shown for this vehicle in myChevrolet. Candidate coverage includes start/cancel, door lock/unlock, horn/lights and cancellation, trunk lock/unlock, location, diagnostics, vehicle/account details, subscriptions, recalls, warranty, and any other exposed operation. Include parameters and cancellation semantics. Each row records source method, Go method, fixed endpoint version, risk class (`low` for lockDoor/lockTrunk; `high` for start, unlockDoor, unlockTrunk; read-only rows separate), vehicle support/entitlement, all three app surfaces, confirmation, and test evidence. Trunk unlock does not imply liftgate motion; EV functions are not inferred. Chevrolet documents additional controls, such as trailer-light testing, that may not exist in OnStarJS2: record these as source-library gaps and do not declare complete coverage until supported vehicle controls are implemented or an explicit limitation is accepted. [Chevrolet controls](https://www.chevrolet.com/support/vehicle/smartphone-connections/mychevrolet-mobile-app/remote-commands)

Upstream action commands use v3 (`/veh/cmd/v3/{command}/{VIN}`); pinned door/trunk bodies remain nested while alert uses a top-level body and, since 2.11.0, fall back at runtime to legacy v1 (`/api/v1/account/vehicles/{VIN}/commands/{cmd}`, body nested under `{cmd}Request`) on HTTP 400 or 'Bad Request'/'not supported'/'invalid', caching one version for the whole session. Do not port that runtime fallback or the shared cache. Each matrix row records a fixed endpoint version; the default is v3. A row changes to v1 only by a recorded decision after supervised evidence that v3 returns a synchronous 400 for that command on this vehicle. A v3 400 during normal operation is a terminal failure under the one-submission rule, not a trigger to resubmit on v1.

Diagnostics is mapped from the pinned source's health-status read, a single synchronous GET with no item selection. Record its exact endpoint and response shape in the parity manifest. Store each diagnostic element's provider timestamp as that value's observation time and apply the location freshness rules on every surface: no timestamp is labelled freshness-unknown, an old value is labelled stale, and no value is shown as current without its timestamp. Any live-probe hint in the response is informational only and never triggers an automatic vehicle request.

Commands are asynchronous: accepted, queued, dispatching, submitted, succeeded, failed, expired, or outcome-unknown. Persist a conditional `dispatching` transition before the GM request; a crash in that state is reconciled using a provider request ID if available, otherwise reported unknown without resend. Test crashes before network send, after GM acceptance, and before completion persistence. Record intent durably, then enqueue; do not return accepted if neither durable processing nor a recoverable same-ID enqueue retry exists. Duplicated UI/Alexa requests resolve to one operation. A new intentional action gets a new ID.

Queues: one SQS FIFO queue with two message groups per vehicle: `cmd-<vehicleKey>` for owner and Alexa actuations and status reads, and `loc-<vehicleKey>` for scheduled location slots, where `vehicleKey` is the opaque SAM parameter `VehicleKey` (never the VIN). Queue: `FifoQueue: true`, `SqsManagedSseEnabled: true`, `ContentBasedDeduplication: true`, `VisibilityTimeout: 720` (six times the worker function timeout, per AWS guidance), `MessageRetentionPeriod: 86400`, `RedrivePolicy.maxReceiveCount: 2` to the FIFO queue `suburban-remote-worker-dlq.fifo` (seven-day retention; a FIFO source requires a FIFO DLQ). Producers set `MessageDeduplicationId` to the operation ID. Application state, not SQS's short deduplication window, prevents repeat actuation. Queue payloads contain references, not tokens or coordinates. [SQS/Lambda configuration](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/services-sqs-configure.html)
