Exclusive GM access: the worker acquires the State-table lease item `lease#<vehicleKey>` with a conditional `PutItem` before any GM call (condition: `attribute_not_exists` or `leaseExpiresAt < now`). The lease carries a monotonic fencing token and expires 150 seconds after acquisition (worker timeout plus margin); release is conditional on the same token. When the API or Alexa records actuation intent, it also writes `pendingCommand#<vehicleKey>` (command ID, expiry); the worker clears it conditionally at dispatch or terminal state. Priority rule: scheduled location reads yield to user actuation. (1) On dequeuing a location slot, the worker reads `pendingCommand#<vehicleKey>` by GetItem; if an actuation is waiting, or the lease is held by a command, it records the slot as missed with reason `yielded-to-command` and acknowledges with no provider call. (2) While a location read runs, the worker repeats that check before each digitaltwin GET and, from a watcher goroutine, every 2 seconds while a GET is in flight. When an actuation is waiting, the worker cancels the context of the in-flight digitaltwin GET (it is a read; a wake request GM already received is harmless), stops polling, records `yielded-to-command`, releases the lease, and acknowledges. A waiting actuation therefore waits at most about 2 seconds of detection plus cancellation and lease release behind a location read, never a full 30-second digitaltwin GET timeout (Retry policy, GM operation). A command worker that finds the lease held re-reads it every second until the 60-second dispatch expiry. (3) The 60-second dispatch expiry counts from durable intent recording. An actuation that still expires before dispatch gets terminal status `expired` with reason `vehicle-busy`; web, mobile, and Alexa say "The vehicle was busy with another request; the command was not sent. Please try again."

An actuation not dispatched within 60 seconds expires. Status polling can continue only for the already submitted request, within the worker deadline; a deadline alone is not proof of failure at the vehicle. Command/audit records expire after 90 days; incoming request IDs older than 24 hours cannot start a new operation. These records outlive the one-day queue and seven-day dead-letter retention.

Age-derived terminal states: every command-status read (web, mobile, Alexa status intent) applies these rules before rendering, and the worker applies them at the start of every invocation to any non-terminal command record for the vehicle, persisting each result with a conditional write. `accepted` or `queued` older than 60 seconds becomes `expired` (reason `not-dispatched`). `dispatching` older than 150 seconds (the 120-second function timeout plus a 30-second margin) becomes `outcome-unknown` (reason `worker-lost`). `submitted` older than the same bound becomes `outcome-unknown` (reason `poll-abandoned`) unless a later provider status confirms it. Reconciliation on redelivery still runs and can replace `outcome-unknown` with a confirmed provider result; it is a second chance, not the only path.

Worker acknowledgement rule. An unacknowledged delivery blocks its message group until the 720-second visibility timeout expires, so:

- The worker persists the terminal or unknown state to the State table, then returns success to Lambda, for every application outcome: terminal or transient provider error after the in-worker retry budget is spent, provider timeout, lease contention, `reauth-required`, active cooldown, `authority-unavailable`, stale slot, expired actuation, duplicate, malformed payload, paused, and `outcome-unknown`.
- In-worker transient retries are the only retry path for provider errors. SQS redelivery is not a provider-retry mechanism and spends no provider retry.
- The worker returns an error only when it cannot persist to DynamoDB after a bounded retry. That is an infrastructure failure, the only case where redelivery helps.
- A `recover()` at the handler boundary persists `outcome-unknown` (or reconciles from `dispatching`) and acknowledges, so a code defect does not block the group. A Lambda hard timeout is the one case `recover()` cannot catch; it stalls only its own group, and the other group continues.
- On any redelivery, the worker treats an actuation as expired and a location slot as stale (unless reconciliation from `dispatching` applies), records the outcome, and acknowledges.
- `go test ./internal/worker/... -run TestNeverBlocksGroup -count=1` asserts that every non-infrastructure path, including a recovered panic, returns nil after persisting state.

Fault injection and redelivery harness:

- `internal/failpoint` provides `failpoint.Hit(name)`, a no-op unless built with `-tags failpoints`. The worker calls it at `before-send`, `after-provider-accept`, `before-complete-persist`, and `after-complete-persist`. A crash test re-runs the test binary as a child process (`exec.Command(os.Args[0], "-test.run=TestWorkerCrashHelper")` with `FAILPOINT=<name>`), and the child calls `os.Exit` at the failpoint, covering process death, not only a recovered panic.
- `internal/worker` tests build an `events.SQSEvent` FIFO fixture and invoke the handler, then invoke it again with the same `messageId`, `messageGroupId`, and `sequenceNumber` and `ApproximateReceiveCount` 2. A counting fake provider must record exactly one actuation submission per command ID across both deliveries; the final state is one of `submitted|succeeded|failed|outcome-unknown`, with a reason. Run the after-provider-accept crash both with a provider request ID (reconciled) and without (`outcome-unknown`, no resend).
- Store fidelity: conditional-write tests (the `dispatching` transition, token version and refresh lease, GM lease and fencing, schedule-slot dedupe) run in CI against DynamoDB Local (`amazon/dynamodb-local`, pinned by digest, `http://localhost:8000`) as a GitHub Actions service container under the `integration` build tag, with non-AWS dummy credentials scoped to that step. The in-memory fake is allowed only for pure state-machine tests, and a shared conformance test must pass against both the fake and DynamoDB Local.

Web/mobile/Alexa may report accepted/pending, but success requires a GM terminal success response. Target a response within two seconds and a hard five-second internal Alexa deadline; never wait for a full vehicle poll in the voice request. Inside the five-second deadline each step gets its own context deadline derived from the remaining Lambda time: PIN result validation 1.0 s, fresh LWA authority validation 2.0 s (one attempt, no retry), DynamoDB intent write 0.8 s, SQS send 0.8 s; the remainder covers cold start and rendering. If a step before the intent write exceeds its deadline, the request fails closed: the skill says "I could not verify your account right now; nothing was sent to the vehicle" and persists nothing. An enqueue timeout follows the "do not return accepted" enqueue rule. A status intent reports pending, confirmed, failed, or unknown with age. Provider failures remain explicit rather than mapping to false success. Spoken and card wording comes from the `provider-error-voice-taxonomy` table.

The owner removed PIN checks on 2026-10-23. Android start, lock, and unlock use the existing authenticated owner session and one stable request ID per intent, without a PIN or replacement confirmation dialog. The server derives the credential family and LWA authority; the client cannot supply an owner identity or provider token. Refresh and process restart preserve same-family retries. Logout and a new login clear the prior-family retry intent and warn that a command already submitted may still finish. No application PIN, PIN hash, or confirmation-token path is built.

Alexa start, lock, and unlock use direct owner-authorized requests with no PIN directive or confirmation flow, per the 2026-10-23 instruction. Exact skill ID, fresh request timestamps, recognized person's linked token, fresh LWA owner validation, durable request identity, and revocation checks remain required. There is no device-account-token fallback. Console prerequisites are account linking and personalization; mandatory PIN setup is no longer required. Actual Echo behavior remains an attended acceptance check.

Restrict Lambda invocation to Alexa with the exact skill ID and validate request IDs/timestamps. Handle authenticated skill-disable events even though they contain no access token. The skill manifest (`events.subscriptions`, deployed with the pinned ASK CLI, since the developer console does not manage skill events) subscribes to `SKILL_DISABLED`, `SKILL_ACCOUNT_LINKED`, and `SKILL_ACCOUNT_UNLINKED`, with the `cmd/alexa` Lambda ARN as the events endpoint. The handler accepts `AlexaSkillEvent.SkillDisabled`, `AlexaSkillEvent.SkillAccountLinked`, and `AlexaSkillEvent.SkillAccountUnlinked` only if the skill ID matches and `request.eventPublishingTime` is within 150 seconds. `SkillAccountLinked` records the Alexa `userId`-to-binding mapping. `SkillAccountUnlinked` and `SkillDisabled` revoke that Alexa binding and reject any pending Alexa-originated command; they never touch web or mobile sessions. Recheck user authority at worker dispatch. Do not bypass GM safety checks or transplant permissive `DoorOpen`/`IgnitionOn` override defaults. Live actuation tests wait for Jeremy's presence and a safe vehicle state; no start test in an enclosed garage. [Alexa events](https://developer.amazon.com/en-US/docs/alexa/smapi/add-events-to-your-skill-with-smapi.html)

Owner kill switch and dry run (adopted features `actuation-kill-switch`, `command-dry-run-mode`): a State `FLAGS` item holds global `actuationEnabled`, one flag per command family, and `providerPaused`. Default after first deploy is actuation off. Flags are read in authorization and again before the `dispatching` transition, so queued commands also stop; `providerPaused` is the non-destructive provider pause: the worker acknowledges queued work as `paused` without GM calls and without deleting tokens. Global disconnect stays the destructive last resort. A dry-run operation runs the full path and stops before the GM call, recording `dry-run-complete` with the Go method and sanitized parameters.

## Location and data retention

Protocol facts (source: pinned `RequestService.ts`): a location read sends GET `{serviceUrl}/veh/datadelivery/digitaltwin/v1/vehicles/{VIN}?sms=true&region=na`, which asks GM to wake the vehicle for a fresh telemetry session, then polls with `sms=false` every 6 s, for at most 90 s, until `telemetry.data.session.updatePending` is no longer `PENDING`. A call with `sms=false` alone returns the cached digital twin. Upstream does not define field paths for coordinates, accuracy, and fix timestamp; onstar-live-proof records them. Which call a scheduled slot makes follows the locked `slot-wake-policy` answer.

EventBridge Scheduler uses `rate(15 minutes)` with flexible window disabled and direct SQS targeting. The schedule target sets `RoleArn` (`SchedulerExecutionRole`), `SqsParameters.MessageGroupId: loc-<vehicleKey>`, `FlexibleTimeWindow.Mode: OFF`, `RetryPolicy.MaximumEventAgeInSeconds: 60`, `RetryPolicy.MaximumRetryAttempts: 1`, and `DeadLetterConfig` pointing at the standard queue `suburban-remote-scheduler-dlq` (seven-day retention; Scheduler does not accept a FIFO DLQ). The schedule `State` comes from the SAM parameter `PollingEnabled` (default `false` until cost-estimate is recorded). Enable FIFO `ContentBasedDeduplication: true`, as Scheduler requires. Keep the body stable across retries, containing schedule ARN, scheduled time, and opaque vehicle ID; execution/attempt IDs must not change it. The State table additionally deduplicates the schedule ARN/time pair beyond SQS's window. Its retry covers delivery only. `cmd/worker` also consumes the scheduler DLQ (batch size one): it records each message as a missed slot with reason `delivery-failed`, then deletes the message, with no provider request. One provider operation per vehicle at a time (GM lease), with location slots older than five minutes discarded as missed. No historical catch-up requests after outages. Scheduler precision is one minute, not exact wall-clock execution. [Scheduler context](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/scheduler/latest/UserGuide/managing-schedule-context-attributes.html), [FIFO requirement](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/scheduler/latest/APIReference/API_SqsParameters.html), [schedule precision](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/scheduler/latest/UserGuide/schedule-types.html)

Use an on-demand Location table with opaque vehicle key and UTC scheduled-slot sort key. Store attempt/observation time, provider fix time when present, coordinates/accuracy when supplied, freshness, request ID, `schemaVersion`, and numeric `expiresAt` in epoch seconds. Separate a valid observation from a failed attempt. Retention starts at validated provider fix time; if absent, use first-seen time and label freshness unknown. Repeated identical cached responses inherit the original retention anchor. Reject impossible/out-of-range coordinates and future timestamps beyond a small documented clock tolerance. A response still marked `PENDING`/`updatePending` after the deadline is pending/unavailable, not a fresh fix. Slots record their outcome reason, including `yielded-to-command`, `provider-cooldown`, `authority-unavailable`, `delivery-failed`, `paused-by-owner`, and `stale`.

Set TTL to 90 days: `90 * 24 * 60 * 60 = 7776000` seconds. All history/latest paths enforce logical expiry and use `Query`/`GetItem`, with bounded pagination and a 90-day maximum range. Handle empty filtered pages that still have a continuation key. A latest-location item also expires; returning an expired cached point is forbidden. At 96 scheduled slots per day, the bound is 8,640 scheduled observation records per 90 days per vehicle, plus clearly identified user-requested observations and status metadata. Do not enable location-table backups/PITR or create exports that silently outlive this policy. User-requested deletion explicitly removes active/history records; TTL alone is not immediate deletion. Owner retention controls (adopted `location-retention-controls`) may shorten retention to 7 or 30 days; 90 days is the ceiling.

Use a separate State table for encrypted credentials, owner consent, device grants, command/audit records, flags, leases, and rate limits, so different retention/IAM rules are explicit. Five-year credential expiry, 90-day command history, and seven-day redacted operations logs are separate lifetimes. Data reads never scan either table. Location data remains private to the owner; no third-party map/geocoding service receives coordinates automatically. A map integration or public data export is not required by this plan.

Both tables use `BillingMode: PAY_PER_REQUEST`, `DeletionProtectionEnabled: true`, `DeletionPolicy: Retain`, `UpdateReplacePolicy: Retain`, AWS-owned encryption (no `SSESpecification` with a customer key), and no `PointInTimeRecoverySpecification`. Tables have no explicit `TableName` (CloudFormation generates names, so a retained orphan from a failed first create cannot collide). The State table can be recovered only through an attended GM re-enrollment; that stop condition is accepted instead of paying for PITR. `TimeToLiveSpecification` uses numeric epoch-second `expiresAt`; on the State table it is set only on item types with a bounded lifetime (90-day command history, rate limits, five-year credential expiry). Reads still enforce logical expiry. A retained Location table keeps its TTL. Logical IDs (`StateTable`, `LocationTable`) and key schemas are frozen at each table's first deploy: State in `delivery-workflow`, Location in the milestone that first creates it; record the deployed attribute names and types in Verified facts. After that, `go run ./cmd/verify template` fails on any change to `KeySchema` or key `AttributeDefinitions` of either table. Every State and Location item carries `schemaVersion`; code at version N ignores unknown attributes, defaults missing ones, and reads N-1 and N+1 items.

Expose last successful poll, last attempt, freshness, failure reason, and any `cooldownUntil` in the app and the `/status` page. Alerts/diagnostics use structured logs, the dead-letter queues, the status page, and owner email from `cmd/notify`; do not create CloudWatch alarms or dashboards. Each function declares its own `AWS::Logs::LogGroup` (`RetentionInDays: 7`, no `KmsKeyId`) and binds it with `LoggingConfig: {LogGroup: !Ref <Fn>LogGroup, LogFormat: JSON, ApplicationLogLevel: INFO}`, so Lambda never creates an implicit never-expire group. The HTTP API sets no `AccessLogSettings` unless it points at an explicit seven-day group.

Log contract: every operations log line is one JSON object with `ts`, `level`, `svc`, `op`, `commandId` or `slot`, `outcome`, `reason`, `attempt`, `durationMs`, and `requestId`. Provider HTTP status is recorded as a class only. No logger output has a top-level `_aws` key (embedded metric format creates paid custom metrics). `go test ./internal/logging/... -run TestRedaction -count=1` sends secrets, tokens, VINs, and coordinates through the logger and fails if any value appears. Diagnosis uses `aws logs filter-log-events --filter-pattern '{ $.outcome = "failed" }'` on the seven-day log groups, run from a GitHub workflow or an attended terminal. The State table is the source for audit records older than seven days.

## Mobile updates and UI acceptance

On every application opening, check a signed HTTPS release manifest. While foregrounded, check again after four hours. Closed-app behavior follows the locked `four-hour-update-check` answer: under Option A, on backgrounding the app schedules `App.ScheduleNotification` (Fyne 2.8+) for now + 4 hours with the text "Open Suburban Remote to check for updates", and the Go check runs at the next open; tapping that notification does not open the app (fyne-io/fyne#4953), which is recorded, not hidden. Under Option B, mobile-contract stays `[!]` until a Go-accessible background-execution path exists. Persist check state; test process death, reboot, Doze, force-stop, denied notifications, and next-open catch-up. Android can postpone or suppress scheduled work; record scheduled time and actual check time separately. Show the prompt in foreground or a granted notification; never force an activity open in the background. The OS owns installation. Offline checks preserve the installed app and show the last check state. Each open and foreground check also fetches `/status` and shows a banner for the operator-visibility failure states.

Freeze the package ID, tentatively `ninja.jeremy.suburbanremote`, before signing the first release. The signed manifest includes versionName, monotonic versionCode, minimum supported Android/API version, release time/expiry, package ID, immutable artifact ID, APK byte length, SHA-256, signing-certificate SHA-256, and a stable versioned download API route. Fetch it from an allowlisted HTTPS origin; verify Ed25519 signature, freshness, version, package ID, and installed/release signing identity. Reject downgrade, replay, unknown keys, wrong package, and incompatible OS. The release workflow computes versionCode before building: it reads the versionCode in the last published signed manifest, adds 1, and fails if the result is not greater than every published versionCode (Android caps versionCode at 2100000000). A re-run of the same workflow run (`github.run_attempt` > 1) never reuses a published versionCode. versionName is `1.0.<versionCode>`. `cmd/androidrelease prepare` writes both into the rendered Android manifest, the same values are passed to `fyne package` as `--app-build` and `--app-version`, and both are recorded in the signed manifest and the run summary. After the build, `aapt2 dump badging` must show exactly `versionCode='N'` and `versionName='1.0.N'`; a mismatch fails the release job.

Install path (Go-only, browser-mediated):

- The app verifies the signed manifest (signature, freshness, versionCode greater than the installed version, package ID, and a signing-certificate SHA-256 equal to the installed app's signer), then shows the version and APK SHA-256 and asks the owner to consent.
- On consent, the app calls the authenticated API for a single-use download ticket for that immutable artifact ID, expiring in 5 minutes or less.
- The app calls Fyne `App.OpenURL` with the stable download route plus the ticket. The route redeems the ticket and redirects (302) to a fresh short-lived S3 URL. The system browser downloads the APK and offers to open it.
- The browser holds the trusted-source permission. The OS installer enforces the same signing certificate and a higher versionCode, so the installed-artifact integrity check is the OS signature check. CI verifies the artifact SHA-256 and byte length before signing the manifest, and the S3 object stores the SHA-256 checksum.
- `OpenURL` returns no error, so the app records "handoff started" and confirms the result only at the next opening by comparing the installed versionCode.

Never put a release-time presigned URL in the durable signed manifest; on URL expiry refresh only the temporary URL. If a browser download is interrupted after its S3 URL expires, the owner's retry in the app requests a new ticket for the same immutable artifact ID and the route issues a fresh URL; the artifact and its signature are unchanged. Test an installation and resumed download after an earlier URL expires. Manifest signing remains exclusively in CI. A private GitHub release is not the handset download API. [S3 URL lifetime](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/using-presigned-url.html)

Interrupted downloads or rejected installs preserve the working version/session. Publish APKs under immutable versioned names, then publish the signed manifest last after installed-update verification. Never overwrite a signed artifact in place. Roll back backend code through `main` (see Retry policy, backend rollback); an Android rollback is a new higher versionCode carrying the prior working code, signed by the same key. Release-signing key recovery is tested before public key pinning becomes permanent.

Android build and signing recipe (CI only; also used for proof builds; corrected against pinned-tool output on 2026-10-23):

0. Build `cmd/androidrelease` in the secret-free build job. Inventory every private release with pagination and allocate the highest `android-vN` plus one, under workflow concurrency. Set `ANDROID_VERSION_CODE=N` and run `dist/androidrelease prepare` to render the declarative manifest and icon.
1. With Fyne tools1.7.3, NDK28.2.13676358, platform36, and build-tools36.0.0, export `CGO_LDFLAGS=-Wl,-z,max-page-size=16384`. From `mobile/cmd/mobile`, run `fyne package -os android --release --name SuburbanRemote --app-id ninja.jeremy.suburbanremote --app-version 1.0.N --app-build N --tags accessibility,release`. Actual output is `SuburbanRemote.apk`. The original AAB assumption was disproved by run39954154326; do not restore a bundletool path or use `fyne release`, which adds jarsigner signing.
2. Copy the generated APK to `universal.apk` and run `dist/androidrelease platform-sdk`. Pinned Fyne hardcodes its package-path manifest defaults to min21/target29. The helper strictly validates the binary resource attributes, changes only min21->23/target29->36, preserves resources/classes/native bytes, and removes prior signatures. No framework source or Java/Kotlin/C glue is modified. Unsupported input fails closed. Output is `universal-supported.apk`.
3. Run `aapt2 dump badging universal-supported.apk`, then `zipalign -P 16 -f 4 universal-supported.apk aligned.apk`. Upload only the aligned APK and prebuilt signing helper to the separate signing job.
4. Decode `ANDROID_SIGNING_KEYSTORE_B64` to `$RUNNER_TEMP/release.jks` with mode 0600, mask both passwords, then `apksigner sign --ks "$RUNNER_TEMP/release.jks" --ks-key-alias "$ANDROID_KEY_ALIAS" --ks-pass env:ANDROID_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD --key-pass env:ANDROID_KEY_PASSWORD --v2-signing-enabled true --v3-signing-enabled true --out artifacts/suburban-remote-<N>.apk aligned.apk`. Passwords never go on argv; no `--verbose`. Delete the keystore in an `if: always()` step. Output from `apksigner verify --print-certs` (public certificate digest) is allowed in logs.
5. Checks: `apksigner verify --verbose --print-certs` shows v2 and v3 `true` and a SHA-256 certificate digest equal to the recorded release certificate and the recovery-copy fixture; `zipalign -c -P 16 -v 4` ends with `Verification successful`; the APK contains both `lib/arm64-v8a/` and `lib/x86_64/` so the same artifact installs on the handset and the emulator; `aapt2 dump badging` shows `versionCode='N'` and `versionName='1.0.N'`.

Capture clean and grid-overlay screenshots for each web/mobile state. Web baseline viewports: 360x800 and 1440x900 CSS pixels at devicePixelRatio 1, plus 200% zoom. Android baseline: the actual handset and one emulator; freeze viewport, density, safe-area insets, portrait/landscape, font scale 1.0 and 1.3 in the report. Overlay an 8 CSS-pixel/8 dp grid, centerline, and measured bounds; edge alignment tolerance is two logical pixels unless the report explains an intentional exception. Require zero clipped text/control overlap and Android touch targets at least 48 dp. Verify TalkBack labels/focus/activation on the device, not from screenshots alone. [Android accessibility](https://developer.android.com/guide/topics/ui/accessibility/apps)

Screenshot harness (Go):

- Web: `internal/uitest` drives headless Chrome in CI with a pinned `chromedp`. It sets viewports with `emulation.SetDeviceMetricsOverride(360,800,1,false)` and `(1440,900,1,false)`, emulates 200% zoom as `(720,450,2,false)`, and captures with `page.CaptureScreenshot`. Element bounds come from CDP `dom.GetBoxModel` on nodes carrying `data-ui="<name>"`, so no JavaScript is authored or evaluated. Go `image/draw` renders the grid, centerline, and outlines; the alignment verdict is computed numerically and written to the report, and the PNG is evidence only.
- Android: capture with `adb exec-out screencap -p`, converting px to dp from `adb shell wm density` and `adb shell wm size`. Widget bounds come from the proven `mobile-contract / ui-bounds-source` (for example a debug-only `uidump` build tag that writes the Fyne widget tree to app-private storage); the release APK must be shown not to contain it. Unit goldens use `fyne.io/fyne/v2/test`.
- Font scale: run `adb shell settings put system font_scale 1.3`; the report records whether measured text height changed versus 1.0. If it did not, mobile-contract records a feasibility failure, and mobile-client acceptance needs an in-app text-size setting verified at 1.3x.

Required states: login/return/error; initial permissions and denial/settings recovery; dashboard; every command family and confirmation; queued/succeeded/failed/unknown/expired result; location/history and stale/empty state; diagnostic values with stale or missing timestamps; offline/revoked login; provider cooldown/reauth/paused banners; update available/handoff started/rejected/failed/installed. Use synthetic data. Each evidence pair has app version, screen/state, viewport/density/font settings, and measured pass/fail annotations. Missing clean/overlay pairs fail acceptance. No real tokens, VINs, or coordinates in published evidence. Native capture/automation on OFFICEPC still requires the applicable user permission; prefer CI emulators or user-operated capture.

## implementation-readiness — Resolve feasibility before building the product

depends on: none

All future shell commands run from `C:\dev\Suburban-Remote` locally, or `$GITHUB_WORKSPACE` in Linux GitHub runners. Named `cmd/verify` checks below are a small Go acceptance command built with each affected feature; they validate explicit assertions and evidence, not a generic new test platform. Commands and test packages do not exist in the bootstrap repository. Record measured results as they are implemented; a planned command is not a passing test. From onstar-feasibility onward, a DoD that names `go test <packages>` passes only when every named package contains at least one test: `go test -list . <packages>` must list at least one test per package, and an `ok ... [no test files]` result fails the gate (the pipeline-foundation `go test ./...` gate is exempt because it runs before feature code exists). Each `cmd/verify` mode ships with `cmd/verify/testdata/<mode>/bad.json` and `cmd/verify/testdata/<mode>/good.json`; `go test ./cmd/verify/... -count=1` asserts non-zero exit on the bad fixture and zero on the good one, and every DoD that uses a `cmd/verify` mode also requires that package's tests to pass. A fixture item never counts as live evidence. Dependency resolution: an item's own `depends on:` line is authoritative; an item without one inherits its milestone's line, and a milestone without one inherits its workstream's line.

### verify-report-envelope — One minimal evidence envelope for every cmd/verify mode

depends on: none (it precedes readiness-preflight, whose definition of done writes an envelope report)

- [x] report-envelope — Create the root `go.mod` (`github.com/JeremyProffittOrg/suburban-remote`, pinned `go` and `toolchain` directives). No workflow exists yet, so pushing this code deploys nothing. In `internal/verify`, add the Go type `Report{SchemaVersion:"1", Mode, Commit, RunID, GeneratedAt, Checks []Check}` and `Check{Name, Required bool, Status "pass"|"fail"|"skip"|"blocked", Measured bool, Expected, Actual, Evidence []{Path, SHA256}, Notes}`. Every mode that takes `--report` writes this envelope, with milestone-specific results as named checks (for example onstar `auth-method`, `refresh-proof`, `cold-invoke-2`; web-ui `screen-pair/<state>/<viewport>`). Each mode sets its required check names in Go code and exits non-zero when a required check is missing, not `pass`, or `Measured=false`. A fixture or declared value never satisfies a DoD. Before writing, the command runs the canary and redaction checks on the report and fails on any match. The Execution log records report path, SHA-256, and CI run ID.
- [x] verify-negative-fixtures — `go test ./cmd/verify/... -count=1` passes, including one bad-fixture and one good-fixture assertion for each implemented mode (at first only `preflight`; each later mode adds its pair).
- [x] report-envelope-tests — `go test ./internal/verify/... -count=1` exits 0, covering a missing required check, a declared-only check, a canary leak, and a valid report. No separate test platform, fixture tree, or JSON Schema file.

Definition of done: locally (no CI exists yet), `GOWORK=off go test ./internal/verify/... ./cmd/verify/... -count=1` exits 0 and `go run ./cmd/verify preflight --report artifacts/preflight.json` writes a report in the envelope format. pipeline-foundation later re-runs these tests in CI.

depends on: verify-report-envelope

- [x] locked-platform-choices — Record all four user answers above; preserve Go port, Android APK, Go-only authored code, and supervised actuation.
- [x] operator-decision-batch — Ask every question in "Open decisions for Jeremy" in one batch and append each answer verbatim, with its date, under Locked decisions. Done when each named decision has a dated answer there.
- [x] account-preconditions — Verify exact repository/default branch, names-only GitHub secret access, configured AWS account/role variables, certificate/domain/region metadata, existing Amazon developer access, GM entitlement, vehicle in the myChevrolet garage with OnStar active under the owner (not shared) account, target handset, and recovery destination. Confirm, by names and status only, that the Echo devices and Alexa app profile are registered to the same Amazon account email as the developer account that owns `ASK_VENDOR_ID`. Confirm the SES identity for `NOTIFY_FROM` by name/status only. Actual OIDC assumption is proved in pipeline-foundation after its workflow exists. Inspect `go version`, `sam --version`, `gh --version`, and `ask --version`; pin needed tool versions in CI, including Android NDK r28+, platform 36, and build-tools 35+ providing `zipalign` and `apksigner`. Name the pre-deploy precheck commands from delivery-workflow (stack status, ACM certificate, Route53 record).
- [x] unattended-preconditions — Identify the attended consent steps in credential-bootstrap and onstar-feasibility; validate no unexpected terminal prompt, runner timeout, network availability, and session duration. Use GitHub-hosted CI for long checks. Do not create local timers or scheduled jobs.

Definition of done: `git remote get-url origin` equals the verified repository; `gh secret list --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --json name,updatedAt` succeeds without values; future `go run ./cmd/verify preflight --report artifacts/preflight.json` exits 0 only when each prerequisite row has status `pass|blocked`, an evidence field naming the exact command, query, or console page plus its observed non-secret result and an absolute date, and, for attended steps, a named owner (Jeremy) and the dependent milestone; exit 0 requires every row non-empty, no row marked pass without evidence, and every open decision answered. Do not require the not-yet-created CI workflow or GM helper to have run. A missing item blocks only its dependents.

### pipeline-foundation — Enable early proof through the required delivery path

depends on: readiness-preflight

- [~] delivery-workflow — Deliver the minimal deployable stack and workflow as production-only infrastructure (no staging stack; deploy.md), using the existing OIDC role/variables and least-privilege runtime roles, with no enabled vehicle actuation or polling, through these children:
  - [~] delivery-workflow / sam-skeleton — The `mobile/` go.mod (the root go.mod already exists from verify-report-envelope), `template.yaml`, `samconfig.toml`, State table, FIFO queue, worker FIFO DLQ, scheduler standard DLQ, `SchedulerExecutionRole`, the schedule with `PollingEnabled=false` (State `DISABLED`), per-function roles and seven-day log groups bound with `LoggingConfig`, and HTTP API `DefaultRouteSettings: {ThrottlingBurstLimit: 20, ThrottlingRateLimit: 10}`. `samconfig.toml` `[default.deploy.parameters]` sets `tags = "Project=Suburban-Remote CostCenter=home-iot Environment=prod ManagedBy=sam DeployedVia=github-actions Owner=jeremy"` (CostCenter per the locked `cost-center-tag` answer) and `parameter_overrides = "PollingEnabled=false VehicleKey=<opaque generated key, never the VIN>"`. After the first CI run prints them, record in Verified facts which certificate variable is the us-east-1 certificate, the lowercased `DOMAIN_NAME` value, and the deployed State table key attribute names and types. Done when `sam validate --lint` and `sam build` pass and `go run ./cmd/verify template` exits 0.
  - [x] delivery-workflow / baseline-routes — `/healthz` (HTTP 200, JSON `{"version":"<40-hex commit SHA baked in at build>","built":"<RFC3339 UTC>"}`), `/privacy`, and LWA callback rejection. `/healthz` and `/privacy` return at most 1 KB of static content and never call GM or DynamoDB; callback routes reject a missing or malformed state without a DynamoDB call and otherwise do at most one conditional lookup. Done when `GOWORK=off go test ./internal/web/... -run TestBaselineRoutes -count=1` passes.
  - [x] delivery-workflow / deploy-yml — `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` only. Pre-deploy prechecks: `aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name suburban-remote --region us-east-1 --query 'Stacks[0].StackStatus' --output text` (continue when absent or `CREATE_COMPLETE`, `UPDATE_COMPLETE`, `UPDATE_ROLLBACK_COMPLETE`; on `ROLLBACK_COMPLETE` delete and `aws cloudformation wait stack-delete-complete` only if `failed-create-stack-delete` is authorized, otherwise fail and mark `[!]`; on any other `*_FAILED` or `*_IN_PROGRESS` print status and fail); `aws acm describe-certificate --region us-east-1 --certificate-arn "$CERTIFICATE_ARN" --query 'Certificate.[Status,DomainName,SubjectAlternativeNames]' --output json` must show `ISSUED` and a name covering `suburban.jeremy.ninja`; the Route53 record for `suburban.jeremy.ninja.` in `HOSTED_ZONE_ID` is absent or already an alias to this API; the parameter helper lowercases `DOMAIN_NAME`. A failed precheck is a deterministic failure under the three-attempt ceiling. If the deploy returns `AccessDenied` on `iam:PassRole` or `scheduler:CreateSchedule`, mark `delivery-workflow / scheduler-permission` `[!]` quoting the exact denied action and resource; the fix is in `infra/github-oidc-deploy.yaml` of `JeremyProffittOrg/credential-rotation` and only Jeremy can apply it; continue Go and fixture work meanwhile. Done when `gh run watch RUN_ID --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --exit-status` exits 0.
  - [x] delivery-workflow / scheduler-permission — the first stack proves that `gha-deploy` can pass `SchedulerExecutionRole` to `scheduler.amazonaws.com` and create the disabled schedule. Done when the deploy-yml run creates `AWS::Scheduler::Schedule` without `AccessDenied`; otherwise `[!]` as described in deploy-yml.
  - [x] delivery-workflow / stack-repair — A `workflow_dispatch`-only job `stack-repair` in `deploy.yml` using the same OIDC role (see Retry policy, backend rollback). Done when its describe-only mode run prints the stack status and exits 0.
- [x] private-parameter-delivery — Prove the key-injection mechanism with synthetic canaries before real keys exist, without secret argv, logs, or artifacts. In Linux CI run `sam build` and `sam package --s3-bucket "$CLOUDFORMATION_S3_BUCKET" --s3-prefix suburban-remote --output-template-file packaged.yaml` (never `--resolve-s3`), then `cmd/paramwriter` streams JSON to `aws cloudformation deploy --parameter-overrides file:///dev/stdin --template-file packaged.yaml --stack-name suburban-remote --capabilities CAPABILITY_IAM --region us-east-1 --no-fail-on-empty-changeset --s3-bucket "$CLOUDFORMATION_S3_BUCKET" --s3-prefix suburban-remote --tags <samconfig tags> --no-cli-pager` (no `--debug`). The same helper reads stack tags and the non-secret `parameter_overrides` (including `PollingEnabled` and `VehicleKey`) from `samconfig.toml` `[default.deploy.parameters]`, merges them with repository variables and step-environment secrets into the one stdin parameter JSON, and supplies the tag arguments. `GOWORK=off go test ./cmd/paramwriter/... -count=1` asserts that every samconfig `parameter_overrides` key reaches the JSON with its value and that no secret appears in tags or argv. So flipping `PollingEnabled` in `samconfig.toml` and pushing changes the schedule `State` in the next deploy. This deploys the SAM-packaged template through GitHub, never locally. Secrets enter only the relevant step environment; parameters use `NoEcho` and `Ref`, never Outputs/Metadata/resource names. Before any other output, every Go step that handles a secret issues `::add-mask::` for each line of the value and of its base64, JSON-escaped, and URL-encoded forms; parameter JSON goes only through the child stdin pipe, never to disk; on failure only the exit code and redacted CloudFormation error text print. The canary proof includes one forced deploy failure (for example an invalid parameter type), and the canary verifier asserts the canary is absent in every encoded form from both runs' logs. Prove stdin support with the pinned AWS CLI. Canary keys protect no user data; real keys arrive in credential-bootstrap. [CLI JSON parameters](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cli/latest/reference/cloudformation/deploy.html), [NoEcho limits](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSCloudFormation/latest/UserGuide/parameters-section-structure.html)
- [~] safe-workflow-policy — Pin action SHAs and build dependencies; grant `id-token: write` and `contents: read` only where needed. Use a production concurrency group with deployment cancellation disabled, no secret-bearing untrusted PR jobs, and explicit build/deploy timeouts. Split the workflow into jobs. `build` has `permissions: contents: read` only, no secrets and no `id-token`; it sets `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, runs at the repository root `GOWORK=off CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm64 go vet ./...` (a leaked Fyne import fails here), the test step from Retry policy, the `integration`-tagged tests against the DynamoDB Local service container, `go run ./cmd/verify template`, `sam build`, and the build of the committed `cmd/` helpers, then uploads artifacts. `deploy` and the Android/manifest release job each `needs: build` and download those artifacts; only they get `id-token: write` or secrets, and they never run `go test` or compile modules from source. A `pull_request` or `pull_request_target` job never references secrets or `id-token`. Read actual repo variables rather than copying sample identifiers.
- [x] log-retention-reconcile — `.github/workflows/log-retention.yml` is a `workflow_dispatch`-only job on `main` assuming `vars.AWS_DEPLOY_ROLE_ARN`. It lists regions with `aws ec2 describe-regions --filters Name=opt-in-status,Values=opt-in-not-required,opted-in --query 'Regions[].RegionName' --output text`, runs `aws logs describe-log-groups --region <r> --query 'logGroups[?retentionInDays!=`7`].logGroupName' --output text` in each, applies `aws logs put-retention-policy --region <r> --log-group-name <name> --retention-in-days 7` to each group found, prints names and before/after retention (no log contents), and exits non-zero if any call fails. If the role lacks `ec2:DescribeRegions`, `logs:DescribeLogGroups`, or `logs:PutRetentionPolicy`, mark `[!]` and fix the role through the deploy.md role process. Run it once after delivery-workflow succeeds; record run ID and output in the Execution log. It never runs on push.

Definition of done: CI `aws sts get-caller-identity --query Account --output text` returns `759775734231`; `sam validate --lint`, `sam build`, `GOWORK=off go test ./... -count=1` at the repository root, and `go run ./cmd/verify template` exit 0; the exact pushed commit's workflow reaches success; `test "$(curl --fail --silent --show-error https://suburban.jeremy.ninja/healthz | jq -r .version)" = "$(git rev-parse HEAD)"` exits 0. Future `go run ./cmd/verify deployment --mode canary` exits 0 only when canaries are absent in every encoded form from argv/artifacts/logs of both the successful and the forced-failure run, and runtime self-tests succeed without returning key values. A later CI `aws logs describe-log-groups` read in every enabled region reports zero groups whose retentionInDays is not 7. `cmd/verify template` fails if template.yaml or packaged.yaml contains any item from the lint list in Retry policy, any function lacks `LoggingConfig.LogGroup`, any IAM grant includes `dynamodb:Scan` or a SAM policy template, the worker DLQ name does not end in `.fifo`, the scheduler DLQ name does, the schedule target lacks `DeadLetterConfig`, or the timeout ratios fail. No infrastructure rollout occurs in this planning task.

### android-feasibility — Prove every required OS integration in a Go-authored APK

depends on: credential-bootstrap; can run independently of onstar-feasibility

- [~] mobile-contract — Go-authored receiver bindings compiled on 2026-10-22; the earlier upstream-only blocker is superseded. Full reboot-reminder and TalkBack runtime proof remain outstanding. Build one signed APK using one pinned Fyne candidate (2.8.0 or later; accessibility support first appears in 2.8.0) through the Android build and signing recipe, with unmodified upstream platform dependencies and the accessibility build tag. Prove: Android Keystore-backed handle storage through process death/reboot/in-place update with no plaintext/password fallback or secret backup; browser-out/poll-back login completes on warm and cold launch, with the verifier persisted across process death; permission allow/deny/settings-resume; browser-mediated install (an expired or reused ticket is rejected; a download restarted with a new ticket after the earlier S3 URL expired completes and installs; cancel in the browser, cancel in the installer, and a successful install each leave the running version and the Keystore session intact; record `adb shell dumpsys package ninja.jeremy.suburbanremote | grep -E "versionCode|installerPackageName"` before and after); Fyne `OpenURL` handing a `geo:` URI to an external app (fallback: copyable coordinates); and TalkBack: enable it with `adb shell settings put secure enabled_accessibility_services com.google.android.marvin.talkback/com.google.android.marvin.talkback.TalkBackService` and `adb shell settings put secure accessibility_enabled 1`, capture `adb shell uiautomator dump /sdcard/a11y.xml` per required state (every actionable control has a non-empty `content-desc` or text and a clickable or focusable node), and record a screen recording of swipe-focus order and a double-tap activation of one command confirmation; a tagged build exposing no nodes is a TalkBack capability failure. Update check per the locked `four-hour-update-check` answer: under Option A, prove `ScheduleNotification` fires after process death and after reboot (receiver declared in the manifest), is visible when `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` is granted and recorded when denied, and the on-open catch-up check runs; record that tapping does not open the app and the Go network check runs only after the user opens it. Under Option B, keep the requirement of a network manifest fetch from a persisted four-hour background task (a pre-scheduled text notification does not count) and mark this item `[!]` until a Go-accessible background-execution path exists. Record force-stop as OS-suppressed and prove next-open catch-up.
  - [ ] mobile-contract / ui-bounds-source — Prove a way to get Fyne widget bounds in dp (for example the debug-only `uidump` tag) and show the release APK does not contain it. Done when the bounds file pulls from the emulator and `cmd/verify android-readiness` confirms its absence from the release APK.
  - [ ] mobile-contract / manifest-fuzz — `FuzzReleaseManifest` in `mobile/internal/update` and `FuzzHandoffCode` in `internal/auth`: no panic; bad signature, wrong package ID, lower or equal versionCode, expired or future-dated release, and wrong signer digest are always rejected. Done when `cd mobile && go test ./internal/update/... -count=1` and `GOWORK=off go test ./internal/auth/... -count=1` pass with seed corpora present.

In CI, extract `lib/*/*.so` from the APK into a job-scoped directory and run the pinned NDK `llvm-readelf -lW` on each library; every LOAD segment shows alignment 0x4000. `zipalign -c -P 16 -v 4` prints `Verification successful`. ABIs are `arm64-v8a` and `x86_64`. Launch once on a 16 KB-page emulator image where `adb shell getconf PAGE_SIZE` returns `16384` and record no compatibility dialog and no crash. Record the target handset's `adb shell getconf PAGE_SIZE`. Done when `cmd/verify android-readiness` reports these checks as pass.

Where each Android proof runs:

- android-emulator (CI): GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04`, `timeout-minutes: 60`. Enable KVM (`echo 'KERNEL=="kvm", GROUP="kvm", MODE="0666", OPTIONS+="static_node=kvm"' | sudo tee /etc/udev/rules.d/99-kvm4all.rules`, `sudo udevadm control --reload-rules`, `sudo udevadm trigger --name-match=kvm`), then `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner@<pinned SHA>` with `api-level: 34`, `arch: x86_64`, `target: google_apis`, plus one 16 KB page-size image run. Covers install/update, permission prompts, installer cancel, login return, screen pairs, and Doze/standby/force-stop simulation (`adb shell dumpsys deviceidle force-idle`, `adb shell am set-inactive <package> true`, `adb shell am force-stop <package>`), reported separately from real-time cycles. Pin SDK components with `sdkmanager` (build-tools, platform-tools, the NDK the pinned Fyne candidate requires, `platforms;android-36`, emulator system images) and record `apksigner --version` and `adb --version`.
- handset-observation: an attended local `adb` session over USB on OFFICEPC, under the `officepc-local-actions` permission; not a GitHub job and not a Windows scheduled task. It runs `go run ./cmd/verify android-readiness --device <serial> --report artifacts/android-readiness.json`, with PID, start time, and expected check deadline in the Execution log. It covers Keystore storage across reboot, TalkBack, browser-mediated install, and the real four-hour cycle.

Definition of done: `cd mobile && go test ./internal/mobile/... ./internal/update/... -count=1` plus future `go run ./cmd/verify android-readiness --report artifacts/android-readiness.json` exit 0 only with all target-device evidence. `cmd/verify android-readiness` fails if the uses-permission set differs from `permissions/mobile.json`, the package is not `ninja.jeremy.suburbanremote`, the Fyne version or accessibility tag is missing, or any page-size check fails. Allow at most three proof builds total for this candidate (proof build as defined in Go protocol port and readiness limits). A failed proof marks mobile blocked and preserves the requirements; independent Go backend/web/Alexa work continues. Closed-app update behavior follows the locked `four-hour-update-check` answer, not an automatic fallback.

## location-journal — Record scheduled location with bounded retention

depends on: command-service

### location-persistence — Implement the schedule and retention rules

depends on: command-service

- [ ] cost-estimate — Before the schedule is enabled, record in `C:\dev\Suburban-Remote\docs\cost-estimate.md` a measured monthly estimate for 2,880 slots per 30 days: per-slot provider calls and status polls, Lambda memory and duration measured in onstar-live-proof, the 120-second worst-case worker bound, DynamoDB, queue and API operations, seven-day logs, SES sends, release storage and downloads, and CI minutes. Do not assume free-tier eligibility. The file ends with a line `monthly-total-usd: <amount>`.
- [ ] location-schedule — depends on: cost-estimate (the schedule stays disabled until cost-estimate is `[x]`); onstar-live-proof wake/throttle evidence. Build 15-minute scheduled polling per the locked `slot-wake-policy`, duplicate protection, freshness tracking, retry limits, scheduler and worker dead-letter handling, and the location yield rule; enable by setting `PollingEnabled=true` in `samconfig.toml`.
- [ ] location-expiry — Implement 90-day TTL, logical expiry, latest/history pagination, and no-location-in-logs checks.
- [ ] location-retention-controls — Owner pause/resume of the location journal with optional auto-resume time and retention window 7, 30, or 90 days, held on the owner consent item. Paused slots are recorded `paused-by-owner` with zero GM calls. `expiresAt = anchor + retentionDays*86400`; logical expiry uses the shorter of `expiresAt` and the window. Shortening retention enqueues a purge reference; the worker deletes by Query on the vehicle partition and BatchWriteItem, bounded pages, no Scan. Alexa "pause location logging" works without PIN; resume needs normal person-level linking. Done when `GOWORK=off go test ./internal/location/... -run TestRetentionControls -count=1` passes.

Timeout, cancellation, any coordinate in the output, or an unexplained missed slot is a failure. A green deployment or synthetic TTL test alone does not prove runtime polling.

## Retry, restart, and cost policy

Each running job records its process/session/workflow identifier, start, timeout, and terminal result in Execution log. Watchers detect failed, cancelled, timed-out, and stalled jobs as well as success. Do not run a success-only watcher.

- CI/build/deploy: immediately after push, capture RUN_ID with `gh run list --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --commit "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" --workflow deploy.yml --json databaseId --jq '.[0].databaseId'` (retry up to 6 times at 10-second intervals while it returns empty) and record RUN_ID and SHA in the Execution log before watching. Build/deploy job timeout is 30 minutes each; the android-emulator job timeout is 60 minutes, inside the same three-attempt ceiling; a cancelled/timed-out/failed run is a failure. Fix deterministic defects before a new commit/push; transient reruns use `gh run rerun RUN_ID --failed --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote`. Allow three attempts total per delivery milestone, including the first. The run orchestrator owns this ceiling; disable nested action retries. At the ceiling, stop that delivery and report its concrete failing dependency; preserve independent work. Record `gh run watch RUN_ID --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --exit-status` result.
- Tests: the CI root test step is `GOWORK=off go test -race -shuffle=on -count=1 -timeout 10m ./...` on the amd64 Linux runner with CGO enabled for the race detector, in its own step, separate from the `CGO_ENABLED=0 GOARCH=arm64` Lambda build; the mobile module runs `cd mobile && go test ./... -count=1` in the Android job after installing the pinned Fyne Linux build packages (`gcc libgl1-mesa-dev xorg-dev`). A failing test step is a deterministic defect; `gh run rerun --failed` applies only to non-test steps (checkout, setup, OIDC/AWS, SAM). Before any new push that touches a failing test, reproduce it with `go test -race -count=20 -run '<TestName>' ./<pkg>`; a test that both passes and fails is fixed in that milestone, never rerun, skipped, or retried to green; if the fix is blocked, mark the milestone `[!]` with the suspected cause. All time decisions in tests (15-minute slots, 90-day TTL, five-calendar-year boundary, handoff expiry, cooldowns, leases, backoff) use an injected clock; production code that waits takes its sleep and now functions from the same clock. Unit tests use `httptest` servers or source-derived fixtures and never call real GM, Amazon, or AWS endpoints. A separate non-deploying workflow job (manual dispatch) runs `go test ./internal/<pkg> -run='^$' -fuzz='^<FuzzName>$' -fuzztime=60s` per fuzz target; any crasher is reduced to synthetic, redacted data and committed with its fix as a permanent seed.
- Live verify gating: a push to `main` runs only `go test`, `sam validate --lint`, and `cmd/verify ... --mode fixture|canary`. Modes `deployed`, `development`, and `production` run only from `workflow_dispatch` with input `live=true`, or from the attended terminal. Every GM request a verify mode makes goes through the same persisted retry budget and cooldown check as the worker; verify is refused during a cooldown and reported as blocked, not passed. Each State, command, or audit record `cmd/verify` writes carries `source=verify` and `verifyRunId` (GitHub run ID or a local ULID), is deleted on exit unless `--keep` is given, and is excluded from history and latest reads. `cmd/verify http --mode deployed` and `alexa --mode development` use only non-actuating routes and intents; any actuating intent requires `--attended` and the locked owner-present rule.
- GM operation: provisional per-HTTP timeouts are 15 seconds for command POST and status-poll GET, and 30 seconds for the GraphQL `vehicleDetails` query and the digitaltwin location GET. The worker's provider-operation deadline is the Lambda deadline minus 20 seconds (≤ 100 seconds inside the 120-second function timeout); every per-endpoint timeout times its maximum attempts, plus backoff, must fit inside it (30 s × 3 + 7 s = 97 s). onstar-live-proof records measured p50/p95 latency per endpoint in the Execution log, and the final timeouts are set from those measurements and written back here before scheduled polling is enabled. Read/status work gets at most two transient retries across the entire operation, with 2/5-second jittered backoff or a bounded Retry-After; persist the spent budget before each retry. SQS redelivery spends no provider retry. Ordinary status polling is separate: at most 15 polls, six seconds apart, inside a 90-second polling deadline and the overall deadline. No inner library/SDK automatic retry loop. An actuation has one submission attempt; ambiguity always reconciles or becomes unknown. Terminal provider errors are recorded and acknowledged, not retried until DLQ.
- Authentication: serialize refresh. Invalid/revoked refresh produces `reauth-required` immediately. A 401/403 Access Denied from the GM token, refresh, or authorization endpoints sets a persisted six-hour provider cooldown with `cooldownUntil`, `cooldownReason`, and the sanitized endpoint class; no new password, browser, or GM requests occur during it, and it clears only on expiry. A 403 from a single vehicle command endpoint is a terminal `provider-denied` result for that command only: record it against that capability-matrix row with the time, and let other operations and scheduled location polling continue. Scheduled slots skipped during a cooldown are recorded as missed with reason `provider-cooldown`, and `cooldownUntil` with its reason appears beside last successful poll/failure reason in the app and web status page. Fresh enrollment is attended, with at most two attempts total after diagnosis and any required cooldown. On restart, read persisted cooldown/token version; do not reset the budget. Escalate a provider-required human step to Jeremy, not an automated bypass.
- LWA outcome classification (explicit and tested): (1) `revoked`: the LWA token endpoint returns HTTP 400 `invalid_grant`, the profile endpoint returns 401 `invalid_token` after a successful refresh, or the returned subject does not match the bound owner; this sets `reauth-required`. (2) `config-error`: `invalid_client`, `unauthorized_client`, or a token audience that does not match the registered client ID; this blocks dispatch, does not change owner grant state, and is surfaced as a deployment fault. (3) `authority-unavailable`: a timeout, DNS or TLS failure, HTTP 5xx, HTTP 429, or a malformed body; the dispatch is skipped with zero GM calls and recorded with reason `authority-unavailable`; no grant state changes and no cooldown starts; the next slot validates again normally. Worker LWA validation uses a 3-second per-HTTP timeout and at most one retry after a jittered 1-second delay, inside the worker deadline, with no SDK retry loop (Alexa uses its single 2.0-second attempt). The LWA refresh lease is a conditional State item with a 15-second expiry; a caller that finds it held re-reads the token record every 2 seconds for up to 15 seconds, uses the new token if the version advanced, and otherwise records `authority-unavailable`. Fixture tests cover each classification, lease contention between `cmd/api` and `cmd/worker`, and an outage across three slots followed by recovery with no grant-state change and zero GM calls during the outage.
- Scheduled polling: the scheduler owns only its one delivery retry/60-second age, and its failures land in the scheduler DLQ as `delivery-failed` slots. SQS owns at most two deliveries; under the worker acknowledgement rule the second delivery exists only for infrastructure persistence failures and Lambda hard timeouts. An unacknowledged in-flight message blocks only its own message group for the visibility timeout. Discard stale slots and preserve missed-slot evidence. Restart after a crash by resuming the durable state at the next delivery; an old actuation or location slot is never replayed. DLQ redrive is restricted to safe read/reference records after diagnosis, never automatic actuation replays.
- Scheduled-slot watch: a `workflow_dispatch` job `slot-watch` (GitHub-hosted, `timeout-minutes: 90`) runs `go run ./cmd/verify location --mode deployed --watch-slots 4 --timeout 75m --report artifacts/slot-watch.json`. Exit 0: four consecutive scheduled-slot records exist, each a validated observation or a failed attempt with its reason; exit 3: a slot is missed and the report names its time; exit 4: timeout. Watcher: `gh run watch RUN_ID --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --exit-status`. On exit 3 or 4, rerun once after diagnosis; at two failed watches, mark release-verification `[!]` with the missed-slot evidence. The 30-minute build/deploy timeout does not apply to this job. No local scheduled job is used.
- SMAPI model build: inside the Alexa deploy step, poll `ask smapi get-skill-status --skill-id "$ALEXA_SKILL_ID" --resource interactionModel` every 15 seconds for at most 10 minutes and read `interactionModel.en-US.lastUpdateRequest.status`; FAILED or timeout fails the job. The CI three-attempt ceiling governs; there is no separate retry loop.
- Cold-start proof: between the two onstar-live-proof invocations, force new execution environments with a pipeline deploy that changes function configuration (`DEPLOY_NONCE=<run id>` environment value). This counts against the CI three-attempt ceiling. `go run ./cmd/verify onstar --mode deployed` must find an `Init Duration` field in the REPORT line of both invocations in two different log streams. Do not use sleep or idle time as a cold-start assumption.
- Android feasibility: the real four-hour cycle runs only as handset-observation, up to five hours; record the PID, expected check deadline, and failure/timeout/terminal result. A running test process is not permission to install a Windows scheduled task. Use `go run ./cmd/verify android-readiness --report artifacts/android-readiness.json` for the proof and restart only after an explained correction, within the three-proof-build ceiling (proof build as defined in Go protocol port and readiness limits). The acceptance report includes actual OS-delayed timing; no fake-clock result replaces the real test.
- Mobile updater: a network check has a 30-second deadline and two attempts total, with five-second jittered delay. Next app opening/periodic event is a distinct check. The installer handoff is never retried automatically. Retain the working app on failure.
- Backend rollback and pauses: backend rollback is `git revert --no-edit <bad-sha>` pushed to `main`, deployed by the normal workflow and watched with `gh run watch RUN_ID --repo JeremyProffittOrg/Suburban-Remote --exit-status`. The `workflow_dispatch`-only `stack-repair` job uses the same OIDC role and runs `aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name suburban-remote --region us-east-1 --query Stacks[0].StackStatus`. On `UPDATE_ROLLBACK_FAILED` it runs `aws cloudformation continue-update-rollback --stack-name suburban-remote --region us-east-1` (a `resources-to-skip` input only after the failing resource is diagnosed and recorded in the Execution log), waits with `aws cloudformation wait stack-rollback-complete`, and describes again. On `ROLLBACK_COMPLETE` it deletes the stack only under the `failed-create-stack-delete` authorization; otherwise it only reports. `stack-repair` is the only sanctioned repair path, and repair mode counts toward the delivery milestone's three-attempt ceiling. A State/Location shape change ships in a separate push from the code that first depends on it, so either push can be reverted; `GOWORK=off go test ./internal/state/... ./internal/location/... -run TestSchemaCompat -count=1` proves N-1/N+1 reads. `PollingEnabled=false` in `samconfig.toml` stops polling in one deploy with no Go change; the `providerPaused` flag pauses provider work without a deploy.
- No overall delivery deadline was supplied. Normal deployment costs/restarts are covered by standing authorization. New material purchases, subscription upgrades, and broader infrastructure remain separate scope. Do not send email or other notifications from this planning run; later operator mail follows an explicit request. Application owner email from `cmd/notify` is product behavior, not operator status mail.

Before enabling the schedule, the `cost-estimate` item records a measured monthly estimate for 2,880 slots per 30 days, actual calls/status polls per slot, Lambda memory/duration, DynamoDB/queue/API operations, seven-day logs, SES sends, release storage/downloads, and CI minutes. Include the 120-second worst-case worker bound and do not assume free-tier eligibility. One vehicle and disabled unnecessary services bound ordinary usage; no paid map service is added. [AWS Lambda pricing](https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/pricing/)

`go run ./cmd/verify template` fails the build if `template.yaml` or `packaged.yaml` contains any of: `AWS::SecretsManager::`, `AWS::KMS::`, `KmsKeyArn`, `KmsKeyId`; `AWS::CloudWatch::Alarm`, `AWS::CloudWatch::Dashboard`; `AWS::EC2::`, `AWS::AutoScaling::` (forbidden by `C:\dev\CLAUDE.md`), `VpcConfig`; `Tracing: Active` or `TracingEnabled: true`, a `LambdaInsightsExtension` layer, `ProvisionedConcurrencyConfig`; `Tier: Advanced` on an SSM parameter, `AWS::Serverless::Api` (REST); PITR or backups on either table; any S3 `VersioningConfiguration` without a matching `LifecycleConfiguration` noncurrent-version expiration; any `AWS::Logs::LogGroup` without `RetentionInDays: 7`; any IAM grant of `dynamodb:Scan` or a SAM policy template. API access logs are allowed only into an owned seven-day log group. The ban on serving-path Scan in Go code is enforced by a test in `GOWORK=off go test ./...` that fails if `internal/` or `cmd/` calls the DynamoDB `Scan` API. A Go test asserts no logger output has a top-level `_aws` key. Before the template sets `ReservedConcurrentExecutions`, CI reads `aws lambda get-account-settings` and records the account concurrency limit; when the limit allows reservation with 100 unreserved executions kept, `cmd/api` sets `ReservedConcurrentExecutions: 5` and `cmd/alexa` sets 2; when it does not, record a blocker and do not deploy those functions without a ceiling. Explicitly own all created log groups with seven-day retention. The account-wide retention reconciliation is the `log-retention-reconcile` item; do not claim it occurred during planning.

## Stop conditions (only these)

- A required feasibility proof fails within its fixed ceiling: record the specific unsupported protocol/Android capability and continue independent work, including every `*-fixture` item. Preserving the full locked requirements may leave a dependent implementation blocked; do not silently replace them.
- Credentials, account entitlements, developer accounts, or hardware needed for a required proof are unavailable: name the exact missing input and owner; do not invent credentials or passing evidence.
- A new destructive data change, material purchase, public release audience, or material scope expansion lacks authorization: obtain the specific decision. Ordinary deployments already have standing authorization.
- Higher-priority safety constraints or provider authentication blocking prevents the selected path: stop live provider traffic at the documented attempt ceiling; retain evidence and continue work that does not depend on access. Do not bypass access controls.
- The owner's Alexa devices are not on the skill's developer account and no `alexa-device-account` decision is recorded: alexa-client end-to-end verification is blocked `[!]`. Continue web, mobile, and backend work.

## Persona review and dispositions

Three independent personas reviewed the initial plan, researched primary sources, then reviewed the revised plan. All findings below concern the plan; acceptance tests and real runtime behavior remain future work.

- Integration/reliability (review transcript not retained in this repository; the dispositions in this section are the record): P0 Node/browser/logging incompatibility resolved by the locked Go protocol port and single owner-run enrollment design. P1 duplicate actuation, nested retries, pending location, scheduler ambiguity, and late deployment proof resolved in Vehicle commands, Location, Retry policy, and pipeline-foundation. P1 preflight/OIDC and credential/GM-helper dependency loops were removed: actual OIDC proof follows workflow creation, and GM enrollment follows helper construction in onstar-feasibility. P2 TTL anchor, source/vehicle capability gaps, adapter-version pinning, and Scheduler FIFO settings are explicit. GM fresh-auth/refresh feasibility remains unverified and gated.
- Identity/security/Alexa (review transcript not retained in this repository; the dispositions in this section are the record): P1 owner takeover/token audience, native OAuth handoff, PIN-result validation, and queued revocation resolved in Identity and Vehicle commands. P1 closed-app LWA revocation requires a fresh check at every scheduled dispatch, with no cache reuse. P1 secret argv/artifact exposure has an explicit stdin parameter proof gate; P2 one-use GM seed is removed after import and cannot reappear on deployment. Five-year retention has explicit revocation/expiry and cannot promise five-year provider validity.
- Mobile/UX/release (review transcript not retained in this repository; the dispositions in this section are the record): P1 callback binding and P2 permission-package audit, signing recovery, background/TalkBack proof, and measured screenshot alignment resolved in Identity, Mobile updates, and android-feasibility. iPhone-specific signing/store concerns were removed by the Android decision. The full strict-Go Android capability path remains an honest feasibility gap. P1/P2 expired S3-link risk found independently by mobile and identity reviewers is resolved by signing a stable download route and issuing temporary URLs on demand.

Reviewers must not resolve a runtime risk by marking a fixture as live evidence. A failed Go mobile or GM proof remains visible and prevents claiming the full application is complete. These risks do not prevent delivery of this reviewed plan.

## Review round 2 dispositions

Nine personas reviewed the round-1 plan on 2026-10-22 against primary sources; 79 findings were confirmed and all were applied. Where two findings conflicted, the resolution is named. Review transcripts are not retained in this repository; this section is the record.

- go-lambda-architect: P1 archived adapter replaced by owned `internal/lambdahttp` on Fiber v3 with a two-attempt fiber-feasibility ceiling; P1 single-group starvation resolved by two message groups plus a fenced GM lease; P2 Fyne breaking root builds resolved by a separate `mobile/` module and `GOWORK=off`; P2 worker timeout/visibility reconciled (`Timeout: 120`, visibility 720, acknowledge every handled outcome, `recover()`); P2 test seams, DynamoDB Local integration tests, and named helper entrypoints added; P2 Lambda deadline propagated into handlers with 504 mapping; P3 init discipline and measured cold starts; P3 payload 2.0 contract assertions made explicit.
- security-identity: P1 owner bootstrap defined (`OWNER_AMAZON_USER_ID`); P2 A/B credential key slots with re-encrypt-on-read; P2 owner app PIN and rotating session handles; P2 accepted-risk threat model added and the OnStar PIN moved into the encrypted bootstrap; P2 ASK inventory corrected (`ASK_VENDOR_ID`, built-in client); P2 build/deploy job split; P3 separate Alexa LWA profile (superseded by the user's shared-profile decision on 2026-10-22); P3 queue policy and Scheduler confused-deputy conditions; P3 masking of derived secret forms and a forced-failure canary.
- onstar-protocol: P1 enrollment uses a non-automated browser with PKCE redirect capture, chromedp only as permitted fallback; P2 `sms=true` wake semantics recorded and a wake-policy decision added; P2 bootstrap payload schema defined; P3 v3/v1 fallback not ported, fixed per-row endpoint version; P3 `vehicle-not-linked` instead of delete-and-reauth; P3 diagnostics freshness labels; P3 candidate upstream SHA and drift check; P3 per-endpoint timeouts set provisionally and measured.
- android-fyne-mobile: P1 app links replaced by browser-out/poll-back login; P1 four-hour background check made an up-front decision; P1 release build and v2/v3 signing recipe specified; P2 16 KB page-size checks; P2 accessibility build tag and TalkBack evidence commands; P2 browser-mediated install with no `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES`; P2 project manifest override; P2 versionCode generation; P2 proof build defined and ceiling raised to three.
- alexa-voice: P2 console-only PIN prerequisites and a person-linking proof; P2 account-linking values with the secret entered only in the console (originally applied on the separate Alexa profile; superseded by the shared-profile decision on 2026-10-22, with the secret now also an API/worker GitHub secret); P2 device-account precondition, decision, and stop condition; P2 interaction model and invocation-name decision; P2 skill event subscriptions; P2 skill-shell creation and ARN ordering.
- aws-cost-infra: P2 Scheduler role, MessageGroupId, and early PassRole proof; P2 failed-create recovery and pre-deploy prechecks (stack delete gated by a new decision); P2 FIFO DLQ, `MaximumConcurrency: 2`, and queue attributes (visibility kept at 720 per go-lambda-architect and sre-reliability; two groups confine a stall to one group); P2 `LoggingConfig` binding and log-retention workflow; P2 per-function inline IAM; P2 expanded template lint, API throttling, and reserved concurrency; P2 table deletion protection and key-schema freeze (generated table names added to avoid orphan collisions); P3 tags and packaging bucket.
- plan-operability: P2 dependency graph split into fixture and integration items with `vehicle-fake`; P2 attended bootstrap cleanup and empty-seed behavior; P2 cost-estimate and log-retention-reconcile items; P2 watchers for slot watch, SMAPI build, and cold starts; P3 bundled items split into named children; P3 decidable DoD values and canonical host; P3 milestone depends lines, `/root` paths, and the authorization source line.
- qa-test-strategy: P2 vacuous-test gates and verify negative fixtures; P2 live-verify gating and verify-record isolation; P2 failpoints, redelivery replay, and DynamoDB Local conformance; P2 report envelope; P2 Alexa envelope corpus and latency measurement; P2 fuzz targets; P2 Go screenshot harness; P2 emulator/KVM/ABI/handset split; P3 explicit APK continuity assertions; P3 flaky-test policy and injected clock; P2 redaction scan; P3 deployed location/schedule assertions.
- sre-reliability: P1 worker acknowledgement rule; P2 timeout reserve and ratio check (reserve 20 s); P1 location yields to actuation; P2 operator-visibility milestone; P2 scheduler standard DLQ and worker FIFO DLQ; P2 LWA outcome classification and lease; P1 cooldown scoped to auth endpoints; P2 age-derived terminal states (bound 150 s, matched to the 120-second timeout); P2 rollback, `stack-repair`, `schemaVersion`, `PollingEnabled`, and `providerPaused`; P2 key rotation (merged into the A/B slot design, because GitHub secrets cannot be read back to fill a "previous" slot); P2 per-step Alexa budget; P2 runbook and log contract.
- Completeness check (same date): a critic pass found lost content, partly applied findings, naming defects, and six consistency defects in the first round-2 draft. All were corrected: the adapter fact and the resumed-download test are restored verbatim; delivery-workflow restates production-only infrastructure; Alexa setup status is in the credential-bootstrap gate; every split child names one check; nested children are qualified and fixture/integration items are siblings; `operator-visibility / daily-digest` no longer shares the decision's name; round-2 facts are split into editor-verified and reviewer-cited; the location yield bound now cancels the in-flight digitaltwin GET (15-second test bound); the Android manifest is a template rendered by `cmd/androidmanifest`; `health-notification` declares its mobile and web dependencies; `PollingEnabled` reaches the stack through `cmd/paramwriter`; vehicle-fake's effective dependency is explicit; verify-report-envelope now precedes readiness-preflight.
